# 371: This Is Team Rocket’s Future! (BONUS)

Sinnoh, Sunyshore City.

After spending a few more days in Sunyshore, Johan bid farewell to Flint, Volkner, Maylene, and Jasmine before boarding a flight to the Hoenn region.

He had been in Sinnoh long enough, and honestly, he was starting to miss his own bed.

Besides—

If he calculated correctly, it was almost New Year's.

He couldn't just leave the Indeedee siblings at home to celebrate alone, right?

Sunyshore City Airport.

"Mr. Johan, I have some news. The League just sent it to me recently."

Before parting, Volkner spoke up.

"Oh?"

Was it about the League's reward?

After all, he had helped pacify that giant baby Regigigas. Surely, they would reward him with at least ten or twenty million Pokédollars, right?

"Uh… The Sinnoh League headquarters has officially invited you to participate in the next Lily of the Valley Conference, with no prerequisites required," Volkner said, glancing at his phone.

At the same time, he handed Johan an invitation letter detailing the tournament schedule and basic rewards.

After a brief silence—

Johan finally asked uncertainly,

"That's… it?"

"That's it."

Johan adjusted his glasses slightly, deep in thought.

Was this supposed to be his reward for helping with Regigigas?

Just a free pass to enter the Lily of the Valley Conference?

Did they seriously think that was enough?

Should he cancel his Hoenn trip, wake up Regigigas, and take it on a stroll to Lily of the Valley Island?

Sensing Johan's dangerous train of thought, Volkner quickly added,

"Yesterday, Elite Four Bertha called me. She said the Sinnoh League is still carefully considering the matter. They want to ensure you receive a reward that is absolutely satisfactory."

"You should have said that earlier— uh, I mean, protecting the Pokémon world is everyone's responsibility. Rewards aren't important."

Johan straightened his expression.

Flint scratched his head.

So… was he asking for a reward or not?

"Alright, see you next time."

Waving, Johan stepped into the airport.

After he was out of sight, Flint elbowed Volkner and lowered his voice.

"Does Johan want the reward or not?"

Having spent the past few days with Johan, Volkner had gained some understanding of him.

Without hesitation, he replied,

"He wants it. And he wants it soon!"

On the flight from Sunyshore City to Rustboro City.

"Lily of the Valley Conference, huh?"

Johan skimmed through the invitation letter Volkner had given him, mainly glancing at the rewards.

It was pretty similar to what the Indigo League offered.

However, the invitation did specify that these were base rewards, implying there was a more advanced version available.

"I'm not exactly short on money right now… I'll think about it later."

Johan casually stuffed the invitation into Gengar's mouth.

He still had over five million Pokédollars in his savings.

Cynthia's payment for the Regigigas research would likely be deposited in the next couple of days—at least another seven or eight million Pokédollars.

Plus, whatever the Sinnoh League was planning to reward him with.

He wasn't short on cash.

Over ten million Pokédollars!

For the first time, Johan actually felt financially comfortable.

And—

He had successfully recruited Alcremie for the café.

With the combined talents of the Indeedee sister and Alcremie, business was bound to boom.

At least for a while… money wouldn't be a problem.

Probably.

There was still plenty of time to think about it.

"Yimay~~"

Just like Gengar, Alcremie was pressed up against the airplane window, staring curiously at the scenery outside.

It was the little one's first time on a plane, so everything fascinated it.

From outside the plane window, one could see a large purple face and a small, cream-like face side by side.

—

Kanto, Viridian City. Team Rocket's Base.

"Boss!"

Matori's high heels clicked rapidly against the polished tiles as she excitedly approached Giovanni, holding a package in her hands.

"You're back."

Giovanni had just finished his workout. Wearing a sleeveless training shirt, his well-defined muscles were on full display—causing Matori's face to flush slightly.

"Yes!"

She nodded vigorously, then handed over a neatly wrapped pink gift box.

Giovanni raised an eyebrow at the unusual color choice but accepted it anyway.

Matori, full of curiosity, leaned in slightly.

She was eager to see what Johan had sent Giovanni.

After all, it was a gift from Johan, so she didn't dare open it herself.

As Giovanni unwrapped the packaging, a plain cardboard box was revealed.

Inside—

"Meow… Meowth?"

Matori blinked in surprise.

It was a plush toy of a Meowth!

Johan's gift… was a Meowth plushie?

She cautiously glanced at Giovanni's face but couldn't tell what he was thinking.

It wasn't that Johan's gift was bad. In fact, he had accurately pinpointed Giovanni's love for cats—after all, the boss had adopted an entire group of stray Meowth at the base.

It was just… not quite the kind of surprise she had been expecting.

"Hah."

Suddenly, Giovanni chuckled.

"Huh?"

"B-Boss?" Matori asked cautiously.

"Tell Johan that I really like this gift. He was thoughtful," Giovanni said with a smile.

"Y-Yes, sir…"

Matori nodded, but her confusion only deepened.

A Meowth plushie… made Giovanni this happy?

Was there some kind of hidden meaning behind it?

Giovanni seemed to notice her puzzlement. Or maybe, since the next phase of their operation involved this very subject, he decided to explain.

"You don't get it, do you?"

"Get what?"

Matori's expression grew even more bewildered.

Giovanni lifted the Meowth plush and said,

"This is the next step."

"The… next step?"

"We're going to commercialize the characters from Meowth & Pichu, building brand recognition, attracting a loyal fanbase, and then—selling merchandise," Giovanni said confidently.

"Merchandise?!"

Matori blinked before realization struck.

She got it!

Johan's gift wasn't just a plushie—it was an actual business plan!

The animated series Meowth & Pichu had already built a solid fanbase.

With continued episodes, more supporters and fans would emerge. Eventually, people would want to buy official Meowth and Pichu merchandise.

And if managed properly—

Merchandising could be far more profitable than the show itself.

Plushies, capsule toys, keychains, figurines, stationery, clothing—it could all form a massive industry.

But even that wasn't the real plan.

In Giovanni's eyes—

This was about integrating Team Rocket into the daily lives of ordinary people.

Imagine—

If things went smoothly, starting with basic items like figures and plushies, they could expand into clothing, stationery, keychains, even household goods like water bottles and wash basins.

Slowly but surely—

Team Rocket would weave itself into everyday life.

Once their brand was solidified, they could expand into various industries, producing all sorts of daily necessities.

What was this?

This was a grassroots takeover—a bottom-up strategy to surround the Pokémon League by embedding Team Rocket into society itself!

Not only would Team Rocket establish itself publicly—

But it would do so in a way the League couldn't directly oppose.

Was this just a plush toy?

No.

This was Team Rocket's future!

"...No wonder Johan is so highly regarded," Matori murmured.

At the same time, she felt a heavy weight on her shoulders.

It was clear—this project would fall under her responsibilities.

—

"Like it? Glad to hear that."

"Grateful? Haha, it's just a simple plushie. No need to be so formal."

"Alright, next time I'll drop by for a visit."

Johan hung up his call with Matori and rubbed his chin.

"Looks like Giovanni really does love cats… A single Meowth plushie made him this happy?"

"If I send him a life-sized Persian plush next time, will he be over the moon?"

"This guy's actually pretty easy to get along with…"

Walking through the familiar streets of Rustboro City, he spotted his café in the distance.

Waiting at the entrance—

The Indeedee siblings.

"Ai~yi~~"

"Ai, ai-yi~"

After giving them a big hug, Johan released his Pokémon.

It had only been a few days, but the reunion was full of excitement.

"Yimay?"

Alcremie shyly peeked out from behind Johan, looking at them.

"Ai-yi?"

The Indeedee sister, who had already noticed Alcremie, immediately stepped forward with a warm, gentle gaze.

"Yimay~"

A gentle Indeedee sister, a shy Indeedee brother, and an understanding Alcremie—

The café's lineup had leveled up once again!

Johan looked at the little ones getting acquainted again and clapped his hands.

"I got you all some gifts!"

Bringing back gifts after a trip had practically become a household tradition.

The moment he said that, all the Pokémon's eyes lit up.

Gengar, in particular, was the most eager, floating closer with an excited "Ka-kaah!"

Johan chuckled and began distributing the gifts one by one.

For the Indeedee siblings, he had brought a set of beautifully carved wooden serving trays from Sinnoh, perfect for use in the café.

For Alcremie, he had specially picked up a set of premium cream-based ingredients—something it could use to experiment with new flavors.

For Gengar, he had gotten a ghostly-patterned scarf, something stylish yet fitting for its mischievous nature.

For Charcadet, he had found a high-quality polishing stone, perfect for maintaining the shine of its armor.

And for Ninetales—

"A special brush. It's made with fine bristles designed for Fire-type fur, should feel really nice."

"Oowoo~" Ninetales rubbed against him, clearly pleased.

Seeing everyone happily examining their gifts, Johan felt a sense of satisfaction.

Home.

This was home.

Though he had traveled far and seen much, the feeling of returning to the café, to his Pokémon, to the ones waiting for him—it was something irreplaceable.

As the afternoon sun cast a golden glow over Rustboro City, Johan stretched lazily.

"Alright, let's get back to work. Business won't run itself."

With that, the daily routine of the café resumed, but this time, with an even stronger team.

And who knew?

With Alcremie now part of the crew, maybe the café really would become famous for having the best cakes in Hoenn.

# 372: Long Life (Scratched Out) Immortality! (BONUS)

December 31st, clear skies.

With a loud whoosh, Gengar yanked open the curtains, gazing at the frost patterns on the window and the serene view of the garden and slow-moving river outside. Taking a deep breath, it bared its teeth in a wide grin.

"Ka-kaah!"

Another day full of energy!

Whoosh—

A pillow came flying at it, but Gengar twisted midair and caught it with ease.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Hands on its hips, Gengar burst into laughter.

It had the talent of a baseball player! A mere pillow was nothing—

Just as it was feeling smug, another object came flying. This time, Gengar opened its mouth and caught it in one bite.

Huh?

It was an Energy Cube?

Gengar was overjoyed.

However, after chewing twice, it instantly realized something was very wrong.

"Ka—Ka-kaah! ～(TロT)σ"

So spicy!

It was Charcadet's Energy Cube! Damn it!

Johan let out a long yawn, rubbing his head.

Looked like Gengar hadn't gone out last night—full of energy first thing in the morning…

"Oowoo~(︿)"

Ninetales blinked its deep blue eyes and looked at Johan pitifully.

"Ah, sorry, I pressed on your fur—cough, I mean, your tail." Johan grinned apologetically.

Walking to the floor-to-ceiling window, he stretched lazily, looking out at the scenery.

"Today~~ is another energetic day!"

"Ka-kaah! |*▼皿▼)"

Why are you copying me?!

Gengar, its tongue now even redder from the spice, cried out in mock resentment.

Johan gave it a side glance.

Copying it?

What a joke.

"If anything, you're copying me."

Man and ghost wrestled into a tangled mess.

"Ka-kaah, Ka-kaah~~"

Wrong, wrong, my bad!

Seeing Johan clutching the Dusk Stone he had just bought, Gengar immediately admitted defeat, bowing deeply in exaggerated sincerity.

Johan smoothed out his hair, letting out a cold laugh.

"A mere 'little ghost' dares challenge me, the Mighty Celestial Dragon, wielder of Supreme Dharma?"

After playing around, Johan and his Pokémon finished washing up.

At the large oval dining table, Johan and his family enjoyed breakfast together.

Watching the little ones happily munching away, Johan couldn't help but sigh internally.

Before he knew it, his household had grown to this size.

Aside from Gengar, Ninetales, Tinkaton, Dragonite, Kubfu, and Charcadet, there were also Rotom, the Indeedee siblings, and the recently recruited employee—Alcremie.

And in the center of the table, basking in the delicious aromas—

The Pokémon Egg.

Rustle~ Rustle~

The egg gently shook.

"I have a feeling that if this continues, whatever hatches is definitely going to be a foodie…"

In those cultivation or high-martial worlds, spirit beast eggs were surrounded by the purest energies and rarest materials, ensuring they were born with unparalleled potential.

Meanwhile, in his household, this Pokémon Egg was bathed in the scent of gourmet food.

If it hatched…

Johan shook his head, unwilling to think further.

Just imagining it might give him nightmares.

Oh, and there was also Marshadow—hiding in the corner, increasingly developing a peeping tom habit.

Don't get the wrong idea.

It was only spying to make sure no one was sneaking extra food behind its back.

After breakfast, Johan clapped his hands to get everyone's attention.

Then, he announced the day's plan.

"Today's mission is—Spring Cleaning!"

December 31st.

Tomorrow was a new year!

In the Pokémon world, there were no distinctions between solar and lunar calendars, so Johan simply treated tonight as New Year's Eve and tomorrow as the New Year.

People here had their own ways of celebrating, but Johan—

He preferred New Year's Eve.

Which meant… there had to be a big feast!

And today was all about welcoming the new and bidding farewell to the old!

With his encouragement, the Pokémon immediately sprang into action.

Spring cleaning!

The café's first and second floors, the living area, the backyard garden—everywhere had to be cleaned.

The little ones were full of enthusiasm.

After all, this was their first time celebrating New Year's Eve with Johan. They had to take it seriously!

Meanwhile, the Indeedee siblings and Alcremie followed Johan into the kitchen.

Starting preparations for the feast bright and early.

With a new assistant, the once strictly "No Entry Except for Johan and Gengar" kitchen was now bustling with activity.

As for the cleaning?

That was left to Gengar and the others.

Technically, it was spring cleaning, but to the Pokémon, it was mostly just an excuse to play around.

"Draaa~~"

Dragonite sprayed a stream of water, flooding the entire room in an instant.

Tinkaton and Kubfu—one with a mop, the other with a broom—turned the living room into a martial arts arena.

"Ka-kaah~"

Floating midair, Gengar gently exhaled at the chandelier before wiping it clean, nodding in satisfaction as it gleamed.

"Make way, make way, Rotom coming through!"

Rotom, possessing the cleaning robot, zoomed around the house.

Anyone who didn't know better might have thought it was piloting a boat rather than vacuuming.

"Oowoo~~"

Ninetales curled its tail around a duster and a cloth, dusting the sofa while wiping the coffee table.

According to Gengar, Ninetales alone was worth nine workers—a true Natural-Born Labor Saint.

That is… until Ninetales glared at it fiercely, and it promptly shut up.

As for Charcadet, it was in charge of the final step—

Burning trash while radiating heat to dry the wet floors.

Overall—

Everything ran pretty smoothly.

Except for some minor incidents.

Like the lampshade getting melted by Gengar.

Or the floor being smashed open.

Or the walls getting scorched.

Minor details.

Johan kept poking his head out of the kitchen to monitor their progress, making sure they didn't destroy the place completely.

Night fell.

Johan's entire household—from the oldest (Gengar) to the youngest (the Pokémon Egg)—sat around the table, which was packed with all kinds of dishes.

There was the roast duck Gengar had requested, the cakes and desserts loved by Ninetales and Kubfu, the fire noodles for Charcadet…

And, of course, Rotom's Oil-Dressed Battery Salad—a special dish on their table.

The TV was on, though no one was really watching.

The ongoing New Year's Eve Festival broadcast simply added to the atmosphere.

"Feels a bit off without the Spring Festival Gala… But having these little guys around is more than enough."

Outside, light snow began to fall, symbolizing a bountiful year ahead.

Inside, warmth filled the air as they excitedly discussed their goals for the new year.

"The new year is almost here, so let's—"

Johan raised his glass, filled with freshly squeezed Berry Juice.

All the Pokémon did the same.

Even Marshadow, feeling the atmosphere, stepped out of the shadows and raised its glass of Moomoo Milk.

"Cheers!!"

A chorus of cheers erupted.

Alcremie, though new, couldn't stop smiling, deeply moved by the warm family atmosphere.

The cream on its body released a rich, luxurious aroma—as if reaching peak quality.

After downing their drinks, Johan smirked as he saw the little ones getting restless.

Lowering his voice deliberately, he said,

"Alright then—let's eat!"

BOOM!!

The room exploded into motion.

"Alcremie, hurry up! If you're too slow, Gengar will eat everything!" Johan quickly placed a drumstick into Alcremie's bowl.

The Indeedee siblings were used to it, but Alcremie—

Seeing its helpless, wide-eyed expression, Johan made sure to slip it some extra food.

"Yimay! o(^▽^)o"

The New Year had arrived.

Gengar and the others made their wishes under the dazzling fireworks.

Johan, watching his little family, smiled.

This year—

And every year after—

They had to be happy.

As for his wish?

Jirachi, if you're listening—forget long life. Just grant me immortality!

# 373: A New Year, A New Beginning! (BONUS)

January 1st, clear skies.

A brand-new year, the very first day.

Early in the morning, Johan received a flood of New Year's greetings.

There were messages from his students, including Steven and the others, from academy professors, from Team Rocket, Professor Oak, and various figures from Kanto and Johto. There were also greetings from Sinnoh, Kalos, Galar, and other regions.

Seeing his screen almost overflow with messages, Johan realized—

"I've really gotten to know a lot of people."

Among them weren't just "famous names" but also many ordinary people, including trainers who had once sought his advice on Pokémon psychology. They, too, had sent their well-wishes.

"Happy New Year."

A lazy voice came through his phone—Cynthia.

Judging by her tone, she probably hadn't even gotten out of bed yet.

"Happy New Year," Johan replied with a chuckle. "Still not up? The sun's already shining on your face."

"Mm…"

The sound of rustling could be heard.

But instead of getting up, it seemed like she just turned over?

"I'm facing the window now," Cynthia said.

Johan wiped his forehead.

When did she become this 'clever'?

"Let me guess… you haven't even opened the curtains?" Johan sighed.

"Oh, right."

Johan: "…"

Where did the sharp and imposing 'Cabbage Queen' go?

Did you lock her away somewhere?

After ending the call with Cynthia, his phone immediately rang again.

"Happy New Year, Mr. Johan!"

"Same to you, same to you."

To his surprise, this call was from Looker's partner, Anabel.

Through the call, Johan could faintly hear the sounds of a scuffle.

"You're…"

"Nothing much, just apprehending some Pokémon hunters," Anabel said with a smile.

Good grief.

Even on New Year's, Pokémon hunters were still hard at work.

But then again, it made sense—just like thieves, the best time to act was when people let their guard down.

"That's tough work," Johan said.

Both international and regional police officers had it rough. Even on New Year's, they had to remain on duty and carry out missions.

After exchanging a few pleasantries, Anabel finally revealed the real reason for her call.

"Congratulations, Mr. Johan. Due to your outstanding performance in both 'Operation Snow Peak' and 'Operation Canalave,' the International Police has officially promoted your rank to 'Superintendent'!" Anabel announced.

Superintendent—one rank above Inspector.

If an Inspector was equivalent to the head of a criminal investigation unit, then a Superintendent was comparable to the Chief of Criminal Affairs.

Although it seemed like only a small promotion, the implications were completely different.

Inspectors were senior field operatives responsible for carrying out missions.

Superintendents, however, had officially stepped into the ranks of command and had the authority to plan and direct operations.

In other words—

This promotion officially marked Johan's entry into the management level.

To put things in perspective, Anabel had worked diligently in the International Police for over ten years, yet she was still only a Chief Inspector—just one rank above Superintendent.

And Chief Inspectors already had the authority to oversee covert operations in a given region.

Above them were Deputy Chief Superintendents and Chief Superintendents, who managed entire regions as top officials.

Of course, Johan was a specially appointed professional, different from officers like Anabel who had climbed the ranks through political and operational achievements.

"A bad—cough—I mean, good piece of news to start the year with," Johan forced a smile.

For most people, rank promotions were a big deal, a recognition of ability. But to Johan… a bonus payout would have been way more exciting.

When it came to police ranks, Johan's attitude was about the same as Handsome's.

Still, this promotion would be useful for cracking down on Pokémon hunters in the future.

Especially now that his own household had gained another victim—Alcremie.

After Anabel, more calls kept coming in.

For example, Professor Oak.

"Johan, I'm very interested in Regigigas. Did you record any data? Good, good, next time we should study it together. Looks like I've already got my research topic for this year's conference…"

Excuse me?

Weren't you the one who initially documented it as being less than four meters tall?

"Oh, by the way, did you message Bertha? I sent her one, but she hasn't replied," Professor Oak said, sounding puzzled.

So, the New Year's greeting was just an excuse—he actually called to ask about Bertha?

How long was Johan supposed to be this human telephone for?

Then there was Lance.

Even on New Year's, he was still out fighting crime.

Team Rocket was on holiday, yet Lance was still working overtime in the Investigation Bureau.

At this point, it almost felt like law enforcement was Lance's side job.

And when Johan heard that Team Rocket had actually promoted Lance, he almost spat out his drink.

Other calls followed—

From Sabrina, preparing for the New Year's banquet: "You haven't updated your menu in a while. Hurry up and add something new—Alakazam can't wait any longer."

From Lorelei, half a year away from graduation: "I realized that Pokémon like Ninetales counter both Dragon and Flying types quite well."

From the Team Rocket trio, currently filming: "Happy New Year, Mr. Johan (Meowth)! Sōrī-nansu!"

From Flint and Volkner: "No idea why, but we got beaten up by the boss lady last night…"

From Palmer, just starting overtime: "Elite Four Bertha actually assigned me a mission…"

From Mustard: "Little Johan, Happy New Year~~ We're doing just fine, no need to visit Armor Island~~"

From Professor Sycamore: "Johan, I'm about to publish my research. Want to take a look first and see if anything needs adding?"

From Lana: "Mr. Johan, I think I just saw Lugia!"

From Mallow: "Brand-new 100,000 Volt Pineapple Juice! When are you coming to Alola? It's on me!"

And—

Johan even got a message from Giovanni.

From a very motivated Giovanni: "Happy New Year. Thanks for the gift—I understand your message. This year, we will reach new heights."

See?

This was why Giovanni was the boss—so deep and full of hidden meaning.

Except… Johan had no idea what exactly Giovanni thought he understood.

After that, greetings continued rolling in.

Clearly, this was not going to be a quiet morning.

All thanks to the mass message Johan sent out after midnight last night.

But honestly, chatting with these people wasn't so bad sometimes.

While his other friends could only send greetings and invitations via text or call—

Steven and the others were different.

"Happy New Year, Teacher!"

Steven, Flannery, and Roxanne showed up at his doorstep.

Each brought a prepared gift.

Steven gifted a Metal Coat, Flannery brought a lump of Charcoal, and Roxanne gifted a Hard Stone.

All were quite valuable items.

Clearly, his students knew him well.

As for their return gifts…

Johan had prepared them back in Sinnoh.

Plushies.

Everyone got a plushie. Equality for all.

Steven received a Mawile, Flannery got a Torkoal, and Roxanne got a Rockruff.

"Wait, why do they get cute ones, and I get a Torkoal?" Flannery pouted, squishing her plushie. "Not that I don't like it, but…"

Because Torkoal is perfect for picnics, Johan thought.

But he couldn't say that out loud.

Instead, he said, "Ahem, you just don't understand. Torkoal is one of the few Pokémon with the Drought ability—it fits perfectly with your Fire-type team."

Flannery immediately looked enlightened, realizing Johan's "thoughtful intentions."

"Then, what about my Rockruff?" Roxanne asked.

"Well…"

Johan paused slightly. "You excel at defense but lack offensive power. When Rockruff evolves into Lycanroc, it'll help cover that weakness, especially in terms of speed."

"I see…" Roxanne nodded, understanding his reasoning.

Steven, meanwhile, curiously asked, "So, what about my Mawile?"

"Coverage. Steel-types are strong, but they lack the ability to deal decisive blows against Dragon-types."

By now, Johan had perfected his ability to bullsh— er, convince without hesitation.

And since Steven's team mainly consisted of Steel/Rock Pokémon, the Fairy-type Mawile also helped mitigate his Fighting-type weakness.

"Understood," Steven nodded seriously.

Johan discreetly wiped his forehead.

"Alright, let's go—I'll treat you all to our café's new desserts!"

"New desserts?!" Flannery and Roxanne instantly perked up.

"The café is open again!"

A new year, a new beginning!

# 374: Flint: Help! Save Me!

"Teacher, there's actually something else I wanted to ask for your opinion on today."

After downing half a cup of coffee and two cakes, Steven looked refreshed.

"Opinion?"

Johan raised an eyebrow in confusion, glancing at Flannery and Roxanne.

But the two of them looked just as puzzled as he was.

Ever since Steven had returned, he had been almost impossible to find—probably off somewhere digging for rocks. What could he possibly need an opinion on?

Steven, however, simply smiled mysteriously, stood up, and said, "We might need a change of scenery."

The four of them headed to the lakeside behind Johan's house.

Seeing them move, the Pokémon that had been doing their morning training in the backyard also followed curiously.

What was going on?

Johan shrugged at their inquisitive stares—he had no clue either.

Then—

Steven took a deep breath and slowly closed his eyes.

A light breeze stirred his tie, and with a flick of his fingers—adorned with two silver rings—a Poké Ball appeared in his hand.

"No way…"

Johan's heart skipped a beat.

Steven's fingers moved elegantly, as if playing an invisible piano. His eyes snapped open, filled with unwavering seriousness, as he tossed the Poké Ball forward.

"Go, Metagross!!"

"Kong-gin!!"

A dazzling shiny Metagross landed with a resounding thud, its silver-white body gleaming in the sunlight like scattered stars.

Steven and Metagross exchanged a glance and nodded in unison.

Then, with an elegant flick of his fingers, Steven revealed a pin that shimmered with prismatic red light.

His gaze sharpened.

Bringing the pin to his lips, he murmured with a smile, "Metagross, Mega Evolution!"

Buzz—

A radiant, seven-colored glow burst forth from Shiny Metagross, making Johan's eye twitch.

So it's true.

Steven had really found Metagross' Mega Stone.

And embedded in his brooch—was a Key Stone!

"Kong-gin!!"

A blinding light engulfed Metagross, its form shifting rapidly within the brilliance.

The X on its face expanded, its eyes glowed a piercing red, and the claws on its four massive arms extended, glinting dangerously.

A powerful aura surged outward.

Roxanne and Flannery had to shield their eyes.

Tinkaton, on the other hand, was tearing up from sheer excitement.

Boom!!

The rainbow light suddenly dispersed, sparkling fragments scattering into the air.

Standing before them now—

Was Mega Metagross!

"This is…"

Johan muttered.

XP System Upgrade… Super XP System?

Cough.

He couldn't help but feel a surge of pride.

Steven had finally "evolved" into an unstoppable force.

Hoenn was secure.

"Kong-gin!!"

Mega Metagross let out a mighty roar, its presence overwhelming.

This was now the second Mega Evolution Johan had personally witnessed in the Pokémon world.

Leave it to Steven—once he learned about Mega Stones and Key Stones, he actually managed to find them.

Though, just this morning, Professor Sycamore had called Johan, saying he was about ready to publish his research on Mega Evolution.

It wouldn't be long before the era of Mega Evolution began.

A new world… where Garchomp suffered the most.

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar scratched its rear and pointed at Mega Metagross, turning to the others.

"Hey, who looks cooler? Me or that guy?"

"Cha~(_)"

Tinkaton: It's not just cool—it's amazing!

Goka.

Charcadet's egg wobbled slightly in its arms, as if shocked by Metagross' transformation.

"A Mega Metagross? So Steven really found its Mega Stone?" Flannery was stunned.

Roxanne was equally surprised.

But after the initial shock, she felt a bit… puzzled.

"Why does it feel different from when Teacher's Gengar Mega Evolved?"

"Different?" Flannery blinked.

Then—she suddenly realized.

It really was different…

The scale of the transformation was way smaller!

When Gengar Mega Evolved, it felt like the entire sky had darkened—the sheer presence and intensity were overwhelming.

Steven's Metagross, though undeniably impressive, lacked that same overwhelming impact.

Buzz—

After a brief display, Mega Metagross was engulfed in another wave of rainbow light, as its body shed the shimmering energy, reverting to its original form.

Steven wiped his forehead.

Even maintaining the transformation for just a short while was exhausting.

A full battle would be a true test of endurance—for both Trainer and Pokémon.

"Teacher, what do you think?" Steven asked expectantly as he walked over with Metagross.

Johan smiled and patted Metagross' heavy frame.

"It's excellent."

He still remembered their first meeting—Steven's Metagross had been just a shy, self-conscious Beldum.

Now, it had reached its ultimate form.

Steven nodded.

This wasn't his first time attempting Mega Evolution with Metagross.

And he had noticed the same thing Roxanne and Flannery had.

Why…

Did his Mega Evolution feel so much weaker than when Johan's Gengar transformed?

At one point, he even doubted whether there was a problem with his own Mega Evolution.

That was why he sought out Johan's guidance.

Now, hearing Johan's confirmation, Steven finally relaxed.

It wasn't that his Mega Evolution was flawed—it was just that Johan's Mega Evolution was on another level.

According to Johan, Mega Evolution wasn't just about a Pokémon's strength—it was also about the bond between Trainer and Pokémon.

The stronger the bond, the greater the transformation's power.

"Rotom, did you record that?" Johan asked.

"Recorded everything, Roto!"

"Send a copy to Professor Sycamore."

"Understood, Roto!"

A moment later—

"[Steven and His Mega Metagross—Super Cool & Powerful! .avi] sent, Roto!"

…Wait.

Avi?

Cough…

Johan shook his head, dismissing some… less appropriate thoughts.

"Good boy, Metagross." He patted its head again.

Then, using his Aura abilities, he checked Metagross' condition.

Overall—Steven had trained it exceptionally well!

Any past confidence issues had been completely resolved.

"Kong-gin~ o(////▽////)q"

Metagross squinted its eyes happily.

Afterward, the group naturally began discussing Pokémon training and battle strategies.

Knowledge—when shared—only grew richer.

—

January 7th, clear skies.

The beginning of the new year had been blissfully peaceful.

Eat, sleep, battle Flannery—Johan's schedule was… fulfilling.

The café's business was booming.

At one point, Johan even considered opening a chain of stores.

But then he realized—his café's charm came from the Indeedee siblings and Alcremie. They couldn't duplicate themselves, so what was the point?

Lying back in a sun chair, soaking up the warm winter sun—

"Now this is the life."

Just two words:

Pure bliss.

The Pokémon were also engaged in their own activities after training.

Gengar continued practicing Spectral Thief with Marshadow.

To Marshadow's shock, Gengar was actually making progress by cobbling together different skills.

Ninetales was studying Pryce's Perpetual Ice Barrier.

If it could combine it with Aurora Veil, its defensive capabilities would skyrocket.

Tinkaton was refining its armor craftsmanship, while Dragonite was meditating over a broken jade-green Orb of Grass, muttering to itself.

Kubfu… was the only one who shared Johan's love of relaxation.

Even its training consisted of sleeping.

Which was fine, except that whenever it woke up sore, it suspected someone had attacked it in its sleep.

Meanwhile, Charcadet was holding a thick book and… reading to the Pokémon Egg.

If one looked closely—

The book was "The Secrets of the Earth."

Since Johan suspected the egg contained a Ground-type Pokémon, Charcadet had chosen to study this book instead of The Heart of Fear or Perpetual Ice Barrier.

And surprisingly—

The egg seemed to enjoy the lessons, wobbling slightly as if listening attentively.

"Well… At least it's safer than letting Gengar do the teaching."

Just as Johan was about to drift into a nap—

Beep Beep—

His phone rang.

Glancing at the screen, he saw a strange, scribbly number—

Like tadpoles swimming across the screen.

He almost hung up.

But then, for some reason, he answered.

"Johan! You finally picked up!!"

Flint's voice rang out—full of despair.

Johan frowned.

Flint?

He checked his contacts—he definitely had Flint saved as "Exploding Monkey +3."

The numbers at the end?

That was the number of times Johan had added him to his 'blacklist.'

"...Hanging up."

"NO!!"

Flint's scream was bloodcurdling.

"Don't hang up! If you do, I won't be able to call again!"

"Huh?"

"Save me! Teacher Johan, I'm trapped somewhere—please get me out!!"

Flint's SOS call?

And judging by his tone, he wasn't joking.

"I was finally enjoying my break. Try saving yourself first."

Johan didn't believe for a second that Flint—of all people—could actually be captured.

"Please!! I haven't slept in three days! They won't let me sleep!!" Flint wailed.

"...They?"

"They keep chanting in my ear—

'You're a monkey, you're a monkey, you're a monkey…'"

Johan: "…"

Flint: "I can't take it anymore, Teacher! I've been locked up for three days, and they won't let me sleep! Every time I close my eyes, they start again—'You're a monkey, you're a monkey, you're a monkey…'"

Johan: "…"

For a brief moment, he seriously considered just hanging up.

Then, with a sigh, he rubbed his forehead and asked, "Alright… where exactly are you?"

"I—I don't know!" Flint wailed. "It's some kind of underground facility! I got captured by—by THEM!"

"Them?"

"The Frontier Brains!" Flint's voice was filled with despair. "They caught me and threw me in some kind of training facility! It's HELL, I tell you—HELL!"

Johan was silent.

The Frontier Brains?

Hoenn's Battle Frontier?

…He suddenly had a very bad feeling about this.

"Explain," he said.

"It all started three days ago," Flint sniffled. "I was just minding my own business, visiting the Battle Tower, when suddenly, I got ambushed!"

Johan: "Ambushed?"

"Yes! By the Battle Frontier's security team!" Flint cried. "They said I was a 'perfect candidate' for their 'specialized combat training program'—then they DRAGGED ME AWAY!"

Johan: "…"

"And now I'm stuck in this nightmare of endless battles! No rest, no sleep, just fighting! Every time I finish one round, the next opponent walks in, and they never stop!"

Johan: "…"

"AND THE WORST PART?! They have this AI system that keeps repeating things like—'Your instincts are strong, but your strategy is weak,' 'You rely too much on brute force,' 'A true warrior hones both mind and body!'—"

Johan: "Pfft—"

Flint: "WHY ARE YOU LAUGHING?!"

Johan coughed. "Sorry, sorry… just—so they're making you train?"

"TRAIN?! This is torture! They have simulations, real-time combat, reflex drills—it never ends! I haven't even seen a bed in THREE DAYS!"

Johan wiped his face.

Alright.

So Flint, the so-called "Exploding Monkey," got kidnapped by the Battle Frontier for "special training."

And now he was desperate to escape.

Honestly?

Johan was a little impressed.

He knew the Battle Frontier's training was brutal—but this? Even Flint couldn't handle it?

"Wait," Johan said. "You're telling me you—Flint, the guy who can solo an entire Pokémon League with nothing but instinct—can't get out?"

Flint fell silent.

Then, in a small voice, he muttered, "…I tried."

Johan: "And?"

Flint: "They were prepared."

Johan: "…How prepared?"

Flint: "Very prepared."

Johan was about to ask what that meant—

But then, from the other end of the call, he suddenly heard a deep, ominous voice:

"Flint! Your next challenger is ready. Report to the battle room immediately."

Flint: "NOOOO!"

Johan: "…"

"Teacher! Help! SAVE ME!" Flint wailed. "I—I can't do this anymore! They keep saying I have 'unpolished talent' and 'unrefined battle techniques'—I CAN'T TAKE IT!"

Johan let out a long breath.

Alright.

This was bad.

Not because Flint was in danger—but because if he really broke, then that meant…

The Battle Frontier had actually managed to tame the Wildest Monkey.

Which was, frankly, terrifying.

Johan pinched the bridge of his nose.

"Fine," he said. "I'll come get you."

Flint: "REALLY?!"

"But only if you survive until I get there," Johan added lazily.

Flint: "…What does that mean?!"

"It means," Johan smirked, "if they actually manage to reform you before I arrive, I might just leave you there."

Flint: "NOOOOO!"

Johan hung up.

Then he stretched, letting out a long sigh.

"Well… there goes my peaceful vacation."

—

January 8th, clear skies.

Johan arrived at the Hoenn Battle Frontier.

As expected, the facility was massive—an entire training complex dedicated to elite-level combat conditioning.

"Welcome to the Battle Frontier," the receptionist greeted him.

Johan flashed his credentials.

"I'm here for Flint," he said.

Immediately, the receptionist's expression changed.

"Oh. That one."

Johan: "…"

"Follow me."

—

Inside the training facility, Johan was led to an observation room.

From the large glass windows, he could see a massive combat arena.

And in the middle—

Flint.

His clothes were tattered. His hair was a mess. His face screamed exhaustion.

Across from him stood his next challenger—a Battle Factory AI Trainer.

"Flint," the AI droned. "You rely too much on instinct. Predictability leads to defeat. Again."

Flint: "NOOOO!"

Johan stifled a laugh.

The Battle Factory staff member beside him sighed.

"I have to admit, we didn't expect him to last this long," they said. "Most trainees either adapt—or collapse. But this one? He just keeps screaming."

Johan: "That sounds about right."

The staff member sighed again.

"Alright, since you're here, you can take him."

Johan smirked.

He picked up the intercom and pressed the button.

"Oi, Flint."

Flint's head snapped up.

"TEACHER!"

"Congrats," Johan said. "I'm taking you home."

Flint: "I'M SAVED!"

He dropped to his knees in pure relief.

But then—

A different voice came over the speakers.

"Wait."

Johan paused.

He turned to see an approaching figure—

A man in a dark, formal suit, eyes sharp with battle-hardened wisdom.

Anabel's superior.

A Frontier Brain.

"We have one final test," he said.

Johan raised an eyebrow.

"And that is?"

The Frontier Brain smirked.

"You."

Johan: "…Excuse me?"

"If you want to take him," the Frontier Brain said, "you'll have to beat him in a battle."

Johan looked at Flint.

Flint, still on his knees, slowly turned his head toward Johan.

His lips trembled.

His eyes filled with terror.

And in the smallest, weakest whisper, he muttered—

"Teacher… please don't…"

Johan smirked.

"Oh? So all I have to do… is beat you?"

Flint turned pale.

The Frontier Brain nodded.

Johan chuckled, cracking his knuckles.

"Alright then."

"Let's battle."

The moment Johan accepted the challenge, Flint's face turned ashen.

"Teacher…" Flint's voice quivered. "Please… have mercy…"

Johan simply smiled.

"Oh? I thought you were the great 'Flint the Unstoppable'?"

Flint shuddered.

The Frontier Brain, watching the exchange with interest, gestured toward the arena.

"If you win, you can take him. If you lose, he stays for another week of training."

Flint visibly lost the will to live.

"Teacher… I'll do anything… Just don't—"

"Come on," Johan stretched lazily. "Let's see how much you've improved after all this training."

Flint: "NOOOOOO!"

—

Battle Frontier—Official Arena

The stadium lights blazed as Johan and Flint stood across from each other.

Despite his exhaustion, Flint still held his Poké Ball tightly.

This is it… If I lose, I'm free… But if I win… I'M DOOMED!

Johan smirked. "Ready?"

Flint gulped.

A judge stepped forward.

"This will be a one-on-one battle," he announced. "Begin!"

Flint threw out his Poké Ball.

"GO! INFERNAPE!"

With a flash of light, the Fire/Fighting-type Pokémon appeared, flames burning brightly atop its head.

Johan nodded approvingly.

"Not bad. Let's go, Gengar."

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar materialized from the shadows, grinning widely.

Flint's eyes widened in horror.

Oh no.

He remembered.

He remembered what happened the last time Teacher used Gengar against him.

The psychological trauma resurfaced instantly.

"Gengar…" Johan cracked his knuckles. "Mega Evolve."

BOOM!

Darkness engulfed the battlefield.

The stadium lights dimmed, and a chilling pressure filled the air.

Shadows writhed as Mega Gengar emerged—a massive grinning specter sinking into the battlefield like a nightmare made real.

Flint's entire body trembled.

I CAN'T WIN THIS!

His mind screamed at him to throw the fight.

But—

From the sidelines, the Battle Frontier trainers watched intently.

"He's been through our rigorous training for three days. Let's see if he applies what he's learned."

Flint internally sobbed.

He knew what they were expecting.

He had to at least fight.

But…

HOW DO YOU FIGHT A MONSTER?!

Johan tilted his head. "You're not moving?"

Flint swallowed hard.

"S-Swords Dance!"

Infernape crossed its arms and boosted its attack sharply.

Johan smiled.

"Alright. Hypnosis."

Infernape locked eyes with Mega Gengar.

And immediately…

Collapsed onto the floor.

Fast asleep.

Flint: "NOOOOOO!!!"

Johan: "Tsk. Too easy."

The Battle Frontier staff: "…"

The judge looked at Flint.

"You may recall your Pokémon and surrender—"

"I SURRENDER!"

Flint didn't hesitate for a second.

He grabbed his Poké Ball and collapsed onto the ground in relief.

It was over.

He was finally free.

Johan recalled Gengar, stretching lazily.

"Well, that was fast."

Flint: "I don't wanna talk about it."

The Frontier Brain nodded.

"A battle is a battle. Johan wins. You may take him."

Flint nearly burst into tears.

—

Battle Frontier—Exit Gate

Flint limped out of the facility like a prisoner who had just been pardoned.

"Teacher…" He sniffled. "I've never been so happy to see the sky again…"

Johan rolled his eyes.

"Seriously? It was just three days."

"THREE DAYS OF HELL!" Flint cried. "DO YOU KNOW HOW MANY TIMES I HEARD 'YOU'RE A MONKEY' IN MY SLEEP?!"

Johan chuckled.

"Well, at least now you're stronger."

Flint physically flinched.

"NO. NO MORE TRAINING. NO MORE FRONTIER. I'M GOING HOME TO SLEEP FOR A WEEK."

Johan smirked.

"…Are you, though?"

Flint froze.

Something about Johan's tone sounded ominous.

Then—his phone rang.

Johan picked it up.

"Yo, Steven? What's up?"

Flint's heart sank.

Steven?!

No.

NO.

Not again—

Then, Johan's next words shattered what little hope he had left.

"Oh? You're in Hoenn right now?" Johan's voice was far too casual. "Sure, we'll meet up. I've got Flint with me."

Flint: "NOOOOOOOOO!!!"

# 375: Solaceon Ruins!

What could possibly make a future member of the Sinnoh Elite Four call a Hoenn trainer for help?

Johan was skeptical.

But considering how desperate Flint sounded over the phone, it probably wasn't a joke.

After some thought, Johan decided to make the trip—just in case there was real danger.

Getting from Hoenn to Sinnoh wasn't easy, though.

So, he made a quick call to Steven.

Not long after—

A Devon Corporation private jet landed in his backyard.

Steven, being deep in his rock-digging business, couldn't come himself.

So, Johan had to go alone.

Once aboard the plane, he dialed Cynthia's number.

"Mm… Flint?"

Cynthia paused, as if recalling something.

After a moment, she said, "Oh, right. I think I got a weird call earlier. But once I heard Flint's voice, I assumed he wanted another battle, so I just hung up."

"…Where exactly is he?" Johan asked.

"Send me his location," Cynthia replied.

Johan forwarded the coordinates Flint had sent him.

"Solaceon Town? Solaceon Ruins?"

Cynthia immediately recognized it.

The ruins were located within Solaceon Town.

Solaceon Ruins was an ancient site where people once worshiped Dialga and Palkia.

Sinnoh had many such ruins, dedicated to legendary Pokémon.

Johan also recalled something.

"Wasn't Solaceon Ruins the first site in Sinnoh where Unown were discovered?"

"That's right," Cynthia confirmed.

Now that the conversation had shifted to ruins, Cynthia's interest was piqued.

Thus, the two agreed to meet in Solaceon Town.

At first, Johan thought that with Cynthia on the case, Flint would be fine.

But if Flint was trapped by Unown, he might be stuck in an illusion, making it harder to extract him.

So, Johan decided—

Better safe than sorry.

Next, he called Palmer.

"Johan? Didn't expect a call from you," Palmer said, surprised.

"Did Flint call you?"

"Flint? Who's that? Never heard of him." Palmer's voice was utterly indifferent.

Johan: "…Poor Flint."

"Ahem. Can you check the historical records on Solaceon Ruins?" Johan asked.

"Is this urgent?"

"A little."

"Got it. I'll look into it."

He also tried calling Aaron.

No service.

Probably in some remote mountain, catching Bug-types.

As for Volkner?

"I don't usually pick up calls from unknown numbers."

Johan: "…"

No wonder Flint ended up calling him last.

It probably took him hours to get through.

Honestly, it was Flint's own fault.

If he weren't so… Flint, maybe people would've taken him seriously.

In fact, even Johan had nearly hung up on him.

Most people wouldn't believe Flint was in real danger.

But if they had known for sure, they probably would have come to help.

"Tsk."

Johan took a sip of his freshly brewed coffee, reclining in the luxury massage chair of the private jet.

"Poor guy."

—

The internal structure of Solaceon Ruins resembled a pyramid, built from massive, precisely cut stone blocks.

Its maze-like passages made it easy to get lost.

The ruins were also riddled with traps—falling boulders, hidden stone doors, spike pits…

Plus, wild Pokémon had made it their home.

It was not a safe place.

Deep inside the ruins—

A very exhausted Flint was running in place with his Infernape.

Or rather—stuck running endlessly.

Surrounding them were swarms of Unown, floating eerily in the air.

A strange energy field radiated from them, trapping Flint and his Pokémon in an illusory loop.

From Flint's perspective—

He was trapped in a never-ending labyrinth of staircases.

No matter which direction he ran—he couldn't escape.

And every time he stopped, a random Pokémon appeared—

Electivire.

Garchomp.

Even Gengar.

And every attack felt real.

Flint and Infernape had been stuck in this torturous loop for three days and nights.

No escape. No sleep.

"Johan… hurry up…"

After countless failed calls, he finally got one person to answer.

Johan.

Flint teared up.

"In my time of need, he actually answered…!"

He clenched his fists in determination.

"From this day forward—my treasures are Johan's treasures!

My wallet is Johan's wallet!

My moves are Johan's moves!

My wife is—"

Wait, no.

"Johan's sister-in-law!"

—

Private jets were much faster than commercial flights.

By 2 PM, Johan arrived in Solaceon Town.

As soon as he stepped off the plane, he spotted Cynthia waiting for him.

Solaceon Town was small and peaceful, with a slow-paced lifestyle.

The town thrived on ranching, with herds of Miltank and Tauros grazing in the fields.

Besides moo-moo milk, Solaceon was also famous for its Pokémon daycare center—one of the most reputable in all of Sinnoh.

To the south stood Lost Tower, Sinnoh's version of Mt. Pyre or Celestial Tower—a Pokémon burial site.

"Flint is really trapped in the ruins?"

Cynthia still found it hard to believe.

Flint had challenged her countless times and never won, but she acknowledged his strength.

At the very least—he was stronger than the average Gym Leader.

Johan shrugged.

"He says so. And the GPS points here."

Cynthia sighed. "Then let's be careful."

If the ruins could trap Flint, they could trap them too.

Better to be cautious.

They followed the forest path leading to the ruins.

Soon—

They arrived at the entrance of Solaceon Ruins.

"This is it?"

The entrance was a long flight of stone steps, flanked by statues of Dialga and Palkia.

At the base of the statues—ancient inscriptions were carved into stone.

Johan recalled—

"All life meets others, and from there, new life is born?"

That was one of Cynthia's favorite quotes.

Cynthia smiled slightly.

"This phrase comes from Sinnoh's ancient legends. You'll find it in many ruins across the region."

She gazed up at the ruins.

"This is my first time here, too. My grandmother studied it before. What you see now is actually a reconstructed version."

"That explains how Palmer found the architectural blueprints so easily."

Johan pulled up his phone.

"Rotom, 3D projection."

"Understood, Roto!"

Rotom scanned the blueprints Palmer had sent.

Moments later—

A holographic map of Solaceon Ruins appeared before them.

Two red dots marked their current location.

"Some sections are missing from the records, so the full map isn't available, Roto!"

Rotom reported.

"This is incredible," Cynthia marveled. "A real-time 3D map… I need a Rotom now."

"Rotom is also great at housework," Johan added.

"R-Roto?!"

They entered the ruins.

The hologram moved with them.

Just in case, Cynthia released Lucario.

Johan didn't release any Pokémon—

Because Gengar was always in his shadow.

If anything happened, Gengar would act immediately.

"Lu-ka!"

Lucario nodded at Johan in greeting.

The deeper they went, the darker it became.

"Rotom, light."

"Got it, Roto!"

Buzz!

Electric arcs flickered as Rotom illuminated the passage.

Suddenly—

A horde of Golbat and Zubat swarmed toward them.

Lucario tensed, preparing to attack—

But then—

"Ka-kaah! Ka-kaah!"

LET ME HANDLE THIS!

Gengar emerged, grinning wickedly.

It loved dealing with these pests.

With a flick of its hands—dark lightning crackled.

BOOM!

Golbat and Zubat dropped like flies.

Cynthia frowned.

"The wild Pokémon here are far more aggressive than usual…"

After fighting off several more waves of hostile Pokémon, they finally—

Reached the ruins' depths.

And there—

They saw—

Flint.

Still running in circles.

# 376: Prophecy of Destruction

Forgot to upload.

----

At that moment, watching Flint frantically running in circles, Johan couldn't help but feel an odd sense of amusement.

Like someone should be playing background music for him…

But jokes aside, the swarm of Unown surrounding Flint was an obvious red flag.

"So it really was the Unown messing with him," Johan muttered.

Cynthia frowned. "What did Flint even do to them?"

It was unusual—normally, Unown simply existed within ruins, embedded in walls.

But here?

They were fully awake.

"Let's save this poor guy first," Johan said.

"Gengar."

"Ka-kaah~!"

With a wide grin, Gengar emerged from Johan's shadow.

However—

At the same time they noticed Flint, the Unown noticed them.

Countless glowing red eyes turned toward them in unison.

Immediately—

Johan and Cynthia felt their vision distort.

A crimson haze spread out, creeping toward them like an encroaching nightmare.

"Ka-kaah!"

Sensing something was off, Gengar's playful grin vanished.

Both its hands shot up, eyes glowing with a psychic aura.

Psychic Terrain!

BOOM!

Two forces clashed in the air, sending ripples through the ruins.

Several Unown flinched, surprised.

The eerie crimson world shattered like broken glass.

"They were trying to drag us into an illusion too?" Cynthia muttered.

And then—

Flint yelled out something completely ridiculous.

"WHO THE HELL DO THESE LOWLIFES THINK THEY ARE, LOOKING DOWN ON ME, THE GREAT FLINT?!"

Both Johan and Cynthia: "…"

Even if they knew he was under the Unown's influence…

The sheer audacity made their foreheads twitch.

"INFERNAPE! FIRE PUNCH! TEACH THESE PEASANTS A LESSON!"

Flint pointed dramatically.

"Ku!!"

Also caught in the illusion, Infernape's body ignited, eyes filled with violent intent as it lunged straight at Johan and Cynthia!

Cynthia smirked, glancing at Johan as if to say:

"Leave this to me."

"Garchomp, Dragon Claw!"

Without even needing a command, Garchomp's Poké Ball clicked open.

The fierce dragon let out a roar, its gaze landing on Infernape.

Recognizing a familiar opponent, Garchomp's eyes narrowed in amusement and contempt.

Its claws lashed out—

SMASH!

Infernape was swatted away like a ragdoll.

"WHAT THE HELL?! WHEN DID GARCHOMP GET THIS STRONG?!"

Flint shouted in shock.

Clearly, Unown had tried to use him as a puppet.

But—

Flint was simply too weak to be a threat.

Even so—

Johan and Cynthia had to hold back their strongest attacks.

They couldn't risk damaging the ruins.

That's why Cynthia had chosen Dragon Claw instead of Earthquake or Draco Meteor.

Meanwhile—

The Unown realized something.

Flint was useless.

A quick telepathic exchange later—

The Unown rearranged themselves in midair.

Each time they formed a new pattern, a strange power surged.

Johan's expression darkened.

...You're not seriously about to create an Entei, are you?

He recalled a certain movie—

Pokémon 3: The Spell of the Unown.

In that story, Unown constructed an entire fantasy world, even materializing a fully functional Entei from a little girl's imagination.

And now—

They were gathering en masse.

"Something's wrong," Cynthia said sharply.

She was right.

Unown were an enigma, an anomaly of the Pokémon world.

Each of their 28 forms represented a letter or symbol.

But when they gathered in large numbers…

They could bend reality itself.

The extent of their power was so vast—

Even the God of Creation, Arceus, had acknowledged them.

Cynthia's expression turned grim.

"I'll handle Flint!"

She moved instantly.

At the same time, Johan gave Gengar a signal.

Gengar fired a Shadow Ball—

BAM!

It collided—

Against an invisible wall.

"What the…?"

Johan's eyes narrowed.

The Unown had created a force field.

It was an invisible barrier of condensed space, thick like solidified air.

Meanwhile—

The Unown's movements sped up.

Johan's Rotom Dex beeped urgently.

"WARNING! WARNING! TIME DISTORTIONS DETECTED, ROTO!"

The time display on his Rotom Dex began flickering wildly.

"Distorted space, abnormal time flow…"

Johan's mind raced.

The Unown were attempting to manipulate time and space.

"Cha!"

Tinkaton tightened her grip on her hammer.

She had once held Adamant Orb fragments—the artifact tied to Dialga.

And now—

She sensed something familiar.

Johan clenched his teeth.

Damn it, Flint.

What should've been a simple dungeon exploration was turning into a prelude to a legendary battle.

Johan forced himself to calm down.

No… This doesn't add up.

Could Unown really create a Dialga or Palkia?

That would be absurd.

Even in legends—

Only Arceus had such authority.

Johan's eyes flashed with insight.

He raised both hands.

"Aura, heed my call!"

A faint golden glow rippled through the air.

At the same time—

A faint, ominous energy seeped from Gengar's Rotom phone.

The aura of—

Giratina.

—

BOOM!

The moment the Unown sensed Giratina's presence—

They panicked.

Their precise formations collapsed instantly.

Their coordinated movements scattered into chaos.

Gengar grinned.

Unown clearly feared Giratina.

It was enough to disrupt whatever they were attempting.

Johan didn't let this opportunity slip.

"Why are you doing this?"

He channeled his aura, projecting his thoughts toward the Unown.

For a moment—

They hesitated.

And then—

They began to write.

Letters formed in midair—

Ancient text.

Cynthia frowned.

"I'll translate."

Johan turned—

Only to see—

Cynthia and Garchomp standing upside down on the ceiling.

From his perspective, they were defying gravity.

From her perspective, he was the one standing on the ceiling.

Johan: "…Great."

Clearly, the Unown had warped space again.

"First, put my friends down."

Johan projected his aura once more.

The Unown hesitated.

Then—

Johan felt weightless.

And suddenly—

He was falling.

"Shi—"

THUD!

Fortunately—

Garchomp caught him midair.

"Huff."

The dragon exhaled two angry puffs of air from its nostrils.

Johan blinked.

Garchomp's eyes were fierce.

"Don't even think about letting someone ELSE catch you next time."

Johan: "…Okay then."

To appease the irritated dragon—

He fed it two Pokéblocks.

"Snort!"

…Fine. Temporary forgiveness.

"Are you alright?" Cynthia asked, stepping forward.

"Yeah, yeah," Johan muttered.

Honestly—

Garchomp felt scarier than the Unown.

"Can you translate that?" He pointed at the letters.

Cynthia studied the text carefully.

Her brows furrowed.

"It says…

'A legend shall awaken.

Rage will bring destruction.

The world will return to chaos.'"

Johan frowned.

"A legend? Awakening? And destruction?"

That was not a good combination.

He turned back to the Unown.

"Which legend are you referring to?"

But—

As soon as the message was delivered, the Unown began retreating.

They drifted back into the walls.

Johan's eye twitched.

Damn cryptic Pokémon.

But—

He took out some Pokéblocks.

"Wait! Here's a small gift. Plenty more where that came from!"

The Unown—

Paused.

Then, they lined up again.

"?"

But the moment they tasted the Pokéblocks—

"!"

# 377: Giratina: Hurry Up and Update!

"AHHHH!!"

Flint, who had been lying motionless on the ground, suddenly let out a terrified scream.

The Unown, who had just been happily devouring Pokéblocks, were startled.

They panicked and immediately flew back into the wall, embedding themselves back into the ancient murals.

Johan: "…"

Just when he was about to establish a proper dialogue with them…

"FLINT!!"

Johan gritted his teeth, squeezing out the words through clenched jaws.

"Huh? Huh?! I—I'm awake!"

Flint sprang to his feet in an instant.

He could feel it—

Not one, but TWO murderous auras directed straight at him.

But when he saw Johan and Cynthia standing before him—

His eyes immediately welled up with tears.

"Johan-sensei! Big Sis Cynthia! I knew you'd come to save me!"

Cynthia and Johan: "…"

Their expressions darkened simultaneously.

Because they had both heard what Flint had yelled while under the Unown's influence:

"Who do these weaklings think they are? These nobodies?!"

Hmph.

So that's how he really felt inside, huh?

Even this big, loud brute of a man… had a hidden side to him.

Flint gulped.

"...Uh… Something wrong?"

"Ku…"

Infernape shuddered.

It had caught Garchomp's icy glare and Gengar's sinister grin.

Without hesitation—

It voluntarily retreated into its Poké Ball.

"Ha… Haha…"

Flint forced out a nervous laugh, scratching his head awkwardly.

"Aha… w-wow… um… great weather today, huh?"

Johan and Cynthia narrowed their eyes at him, completely ignoring his pathetic attempt at small talk.

Cynthia soon shifted back to serious mode.

"So… That prophecy the Unown gave us…"

She frowned deeply.

Johan sighed.

"I still can't be certain."

He once again tried using Aura to communicate with the Unown still embedded in the walls.

This time, they responded with friendly and affectionate emotions…

But—

They didn't form words anymore.

Without written clues, Johan couldn't get any clear answers.

Still—

When it came to world-ending disasters in Sinnoh…

The usual suspects were pretty obvious:

Arceus, Giratina, Dialga, and Palkia.

Sinnoh was too dangerous.

In the grand scheme of Pokémon mythos, these four deities were on a completely different level.

If there was such a thing as "Divine Hierarchy", they would all rank at the very top.

"…At least not every region has disaster-prone legendaries," Johan muttered.

Oh well.

At least there's always some "Chosen One" to deal with these things.

If not Ash Ketchum, then Red.

…Speaking of which—

Red should be setting off soon, right?

Flint, still utterly confused, finally blurted out:

"So… WHAT EXACTLY HAPPENED TO ME?!"

Johan, still refusing to let this guy off the hook, turned to his Rotom Dex.

"Rotom, show him what he was doing earlier."

"Roger, Roto~!"

Rotom pulled up a recording of Flint…

Frantically running in circles like a maniac.

Since the Unown hadn't been visibly captured on video, all they could see was Flint and Infernape running aimlessly—

Looking incredibly stupid.

"Uh…"

Flint's face instantly turned bright red.

And then—

Johan delivered the finishing blow.

"Ah… This reminds me of that day in the ruins… the day I lost my youth…"

Flint: "?!?!?!"

"Classic World-Famous Painting: 'The Running Flint.'"

Johan smirked, then added another masterpiece to the collection:

"Or perhaps, 'The Tree-Climbing Flint.'"

Flint curled into a ball in the corner, drawing circles on the ground.

…Truly.

Neither of these two demons had any mercy.

—

After the comic relief session, Cynthia got back to work, carefully examining the murals.

Meanwhile, Johan turned his attention to the floor.

He recalled something from the Sinnoh anime.

In the show, the Unown's rampage at the Solaceon Ruins had been triggered by Team Galactic stealing the "Lustrous Orb."

And here—

He noticed a similar pattern.

Engraved into the ground were three empty slots.

"Water Seal," "Iron Seal," and "Dragon Seal."

Together, these three artifacts could be used to open a mysterious mechanism.

Johan's expression turned cold.

…So Team Galactic WILL come here eventually.

Without the keystone to locate Spear Pillar, Team Galactic wouldn't be able to awaken Dialga and Palkia.

"…Cynthia."

"Mm?"

"I think the Sinnoh League needs to fortify the Solaceon Ruins."

Cynthia nodded immediately.

"Agreed. If even Flint got trapped here, the place clearly needs better security—maybe even a full lockdown."

That was exactly what Johan wanted.

If Team Galactic ever came for the "keystone," they'd run into heavy resistance.

At the very least, it would delay their plans.

Or even expose them before they could make a move.

Johan even left a message for the Unown:

"If anyone comes for the keystone, feel free to trap them… just like you did with Flint."

The Unown hesitated for a moment…

They seemed worried.

Johan smirked.

Honestly—

If the Unown could move the artifact, they probably would've handed it to him already.

—

Meanwhile…

Far away in an unknown dimension…

The Unown's power surge had caused a ripple effect.

For a brief moment—

Time and space overlapped.

A massive, blue-armored dragon, its body resembling a sapphire-plated dinosaur, materialized in the void.

Its metallic ridges gleamed, and a deep blue gemstone embedded in its chest shimmered with energy.

Dialga.

Suddenly—

A pink crescent-shaped blade slashed through the air.

Its edge froze space itself, slicing through the fabric of reality.

"ROOOAARRR!!"

Dialga's eyes flared orange as its gemstone pulsed.

A swirling blue vortex of temporal energy gathered in its mouth—

Roar of Time!

BOOM!

The two attacks collided—

Causing the dimensional rift to tremble violently.

Across from Dialga—

A silver-scaled beast with purple veins coursing across its body stood tall.

Its shoulders bore pearl-shaped rings, gleaming with pinkish light.

"PAAAAAAL-KIAAAAA!!"

With another mighty roar, Palkia raised its arm.

The pearl-rings glowed fiercely—

Spacial Rend!

Another crescent blade tore through the void.

The duel between Time and Space had begun.

—

Back on Earth.

The moment Johan, Cynthia, and Flint stepped out of the ruins, sunlight bathed their faces.

"Ahhh!! Fresh air!"

Flint stretched his arms wide—

Then immediately winced.

"Ow! Ow ow ow—why does my whole body ache?!"

His gaze drifted toward Johan and Cynthia.

For some reason…

Both of them avoided eye contact.

"…Did something happen while I was unconscious?"

Cynthia casually inspected the ruins.

Johan pulled out his phone and pretended to be on a call.

"Hello? Yes? Very important business."

Flint: "???"

Well…

The warm sun. The cool breeze.

He just wanted to sleep.

He collapsed onto the grass—

And within minutes…

He was snoring.

—

Just then—

Johan's phone vibrated.

A familiar name appeared on screen.

[Young Man]: I think… I just felt a disturbance in space-time.

Giratina.

# 378: Johan – I Knew It Was Too Soon to Celebrate!

"I brought you some desserts."

On the grassy field of Undella Town, a picnic blanket was spread out, and at Johan's signal, Gengar pulled out neatly packaged cakes and pastries.

These were all meticulously crafted by Indeedee and Alcremie.

Not far from the picnic setup, Flint lay sprawled on the grass in a completely ungraceful position.

"Mmm~"

Just the sight of these cakes, which looked incredibly appetizing, made Cynthia's eyes sparkle.

And it wasn't just her—her Pokémon were just as eager.

Of course, Johan's Pokémon had their own share as well.

Seeing this, the little ones couldn't wait to dig in, eagerly reaching out their paws.

"Wait a second," Johan said sternly.

"Garrr." Garchomp reluctantly retracted its claws.

But it quickly realized—wait a minute, why am I even listening to him?

"Ninetales." Johan gently stroked the white-furred little creature beside him.

"Oowoo~~"

With a soft breath of icy air, all the desserts and cream instantly became more glistening, releasing a faint milky fragrance.

Indeedee and Alcremie had specifically advised that these desserts would taste even better with a slight chill.

"Alright, go ahead." Johan finally gave the go-ahead.

"We're digging in." Also eager, Cynthia held a spoon in both hands and smiled as she softly announced the start.

Before long...

All the little ones—including Cynthia—closed their eyes in bliss.

Clearly, they had all been utterly captivated by the desserts made by Indeedee and Alcremie.

A gentle evening breeze rustled through, bending the grass and carrying a soft fragrance.

Undella Town was truly an ideal place for retirement. A few Mareep, freely grazing nearby, slowly wandered closer after catching the scent of the desserts.

After finishing their treats, the Pokémon began playing on the grass.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar patted Dragonite's belly and flashed a mischievous grin at Garchomp.

Come on, Dragonite, give us a little dance!

"Draaa? Σ(дlll)"

Dragonite glanced at Garchomp's fearsome expression and nervously swallowed.

"Garrr!!"

No way! You think I don't know how to 'dance'?

With a roar, Garchomp's aura surged, a dark red energy swirling around it with a metallic hum.

Swords Dance!

No problem, Swords Dance is still a 'dance,' right?

Gengar grinned slyly. Suddenly, it extended a claw, and a shadow separated from its body before diving into Garchomp's own shadow.

Under Garchomp's bewildered gaze, the shadow re-emerged, transforming into several small, bat-like creatures that landed in Gengar's hands.

"Ka-kaah~"

Power!

"Garrr?"

Garchomp looked even more confused.

What the heck just happened?

It was certain that it had just used Swords Dance to gain two levels of attack power boost, but as those tiny "bats" flew into Gengar's grasp, its attack boost dropped to just one level.

As if it had been stolen!

"Marsha?"

Hiding under the shade of a tree, licking the last bits of cream from its dessert box, Marshadow rubbed its eyes in disbelief.

No way—did it actually learn that?

Even though the execution was clumsy and different from its own technique, Gengar had really managed to replicate its signature move by blending a bunch of bizarre techniques together.

Marshadow thought, if Gengar kept at it for a while longer… maybe it really could master Shadow Sneak.

"Marsha?!(>_<)"

Oh no, am I just a practice dummy now?!

Cynthia had also noticed the scene.

She looked at Johan in surprise. "Was that… Gengar's move?"

Even Johan was a little shocked.

Sure, he had given Gengar plenty of advice during its training, but he never expected it to actually succeed in pulling this off.

Is this… the power of Uchiha Gengar?

Copy Ninja Kakashi Gengar?

"I… don't think so?" Johan said uncertainly.

"I think I'm starting to understand Flint's words."

"What words?"

"Johan's Pokémon just don't play by the rules." Cynthia chuckled, covering her mouth.

Johan: "…"

"Well, aside from the chubby one, the rest are fine, right?"

"Draaa~~˙(`)˙"

Dragonite, glowing with white energy, shot through the sky like an arrow.

Its body radiated a special aura, making it seem impossibly fast.

"That seems pretty reasonable to me." Cynthia teased.

Johan: "…"

Meanwhile, Ice Ninetales and Ice Eevee lay comfortably in Cynthia's lap, enjoying her gentle massage, while Johan found himself stuck with Spiritomb.

"At least Spiritomb is cute."

"Spaaa~o(////▽////)q"

Elsewhere…

Kubfu had taken Tinkaton's place, sparring with Lucario before sitting down with Charcadet.

Charcadet was mimicking Johan, using its Aura to massage a Pokémon egg.

"Ku?"

"Luca~"

Lucario sat down as well, joining Charcadet and Kubfu in the "massaging team" using Aura.

"Garrr?"

Garchomp also took notice of the egg and curiously approached.

The three little ones immediately scooted over to make space.

"Garrr~~"

As Garchomp observed the faintly trembling egg, it showed a hint of surprise.

"Garchomp says… this little one surprises it," Cynthia translated.

"Surprises…?" Johan felt the urge to wipe his forehead.

"Have you confirmed what kind of Pokémon egg it is?"

Johan shrugged. "Honestly, not yet."

"Maybe you should have Professor Sycamore take a look," Cynthia suggested.

In the field of Pokémon eggs and breeding, Professor Elm was the top expert, but Professor Sycamore had also conducted extensive research in the area.

Additionally, since Professor Sycamore specialized in Pokémon evolution, he had some relevant knowledge about Pokémon eggs as well.

"That's actually a good idea."

Knowing the Pokémon's species in advance would allow him to prepare for its arrival.

For example, getting the right gifts for a newborn Pokémon could help build a stronger bond.

"Judging by Garchomp's reaction, it's likely a pseudo-legendary or a Ground-type Pokémon," Cynthia murmured, resting her chin on her knee.

Pseudo-legendary? Ground-type?

That would mean either Larvitar or Gible.

Both were Pokémon that Ash had either released or left in training.

However…

While Tyranitar's final evolution lost the Ground typing, Larvitar and Pupitar both had it.

But regardless, both Larvitar and Gible were notoriously difficult to raise.

As a Dragon-type pseudo-legendary, Johan knew all too well how much effort raising Gible would take, given his experience with Dragonite.

Back when he first caught Dratini, it was still overfed, so the costs weren't too bad—but once it evolved into Dragonite, its appetite…

And Larvitar? Even worse.

It was said that Larvitar needed to consume an entire mountain just to store enough energy for evolution.

And not just dirt—it had to eat actual minerals!

The thought alone was terrifying.

"So… how long is Flint going to sleep?"

Johan was itching to confirm what Pokémon it was.

He had even asked Matori about it.

But she had only said that she selected the most promising egg from Giovanni's strongest Pokémon, based on the breeder's recommendation. She wasn't sure what it was either.

Originally, Johan had held onto some hope.

But after hearing Cynthia's speculation, he knew he needed to prepare in advance.

"No worries, we can just carry him along," Cynthia said.

January 7th, evening.

Sinnoh Region, Sandgem Town.

This is a small town adjacent to the sea, known for its sandy beaches.

On a hillside near the beach stands Professor Rowan's research lab.

"Johan, it's been a while."

Dressed neatly, with hair combed to a shine, Professor Rowan smiled as he shook hands with Johan.

"Professor Rowan, sorry to bother you," Johan replied politely.

Professor Rowan waved it off, grabbing a piece of pastry from a nearby tray and popping it into his mouth. He then offered the tray to Johan and Cynthia.

Both declined.

"Ka-kaah~~"

From Johan's shadow, Gengar peeked out, its tongue lashing out to snatch all the pastries into its mouth.

Professor Rowan chuckled, unbothered.

He enjoyed sharing food and appreciated those with hearty appetites.

"Johan's Café has excellent desserts; you might consider ordering some," Cynthia suggested, knowing the professor's sweet tooth.

"Oh?"

Professor Rowan looked intrigued.

"I'll have to try them sometime."

His lab had a significant demand for sweets, driven by his own preferences.

Securing this order would be a substantial gain for Johan.

Johan discreetly formed a heart shape with his hands towards Cynthia.

Cynthia blushed slightly.

"You encountered Unown today?"

They got to the main topic.

"Yes, in the Solaceon Ruins."

The two briefly recounted their experience in the ruins.

"Unown are believed to be unable to evolve. Individually, they aren't particularly powerful, but when multiple Unown gather, mysterious phenomena can occur."

Professor Rowan smiled and added:

"However, in this field, your grandmother and you probably have more expertise than I do."

"I've already sent the information to my grandmother," Cynthia nodded.

"We're here today mainly to ask for your help with a Pokémon egg."

"A Pokémon egg?"

"Goka~~"

Charcadet eagerly brought over the egg.

Professor Rowan's interest was piqued. He took out a magnifying glass and examined it closely.

As Johan watched, Professor Rowan carefully studied the patterns on the egg and slowly said:

"One of the parents is a Dragon-type Pokémon..."

Because each Pokémon egg's pattern varies depending on its parents' species and lineage, it requires a specialist to identify them.

A Dragon-type parent...

To Johan, it felt like a tiny, adorable paw was reaching into his wallet.

After a while, Professor Rowan continued:

"I can't be entirely certain, but I'd say there's a 60% chance it's a Larvitar, 30% chance it's a Gible, and 10% chance it's another Dragon or Ground-type Pokémon."

"Could it possibly be... a Trapinch?" Johan asked tentatively.

"It's possible," Professor Rowan nodded, "but unlikely."

Well then.

Johan's faint hope was dashed.

It couldn't be a Zygarde, right?

Seeing Johan's despondent expression, Cynthia whispered:

"The funding will be approved soon."

Johan nodded stiffly.

"I have some funds available; if you need them, I can lend you some..."

Indeed, Cynthia was a lifesaver!

He had celebrated too early after all!
