# 388: A Feast at Johan’s Place! (BONUS)

January 10th, Light Snow.

"Oowoo~"

Ninetales leaped gracefully across the cat towers scattered throughout the house before landing softly on the plush sofa, curling up into a ball, and licking its paws before using them to clean its face.

"Draaa~~"

Dragonite happily rolled across the floor, then found its favorite blanket and buried its face in it, sniffing intensely.

Confirmed.

Ninetales was "feline," and Dragonite was "canine."

Rotom skillfully dove into the robotic vacuum cleaner and diligently started cleaning the floors.

Charcadet sat in the middle of the living room on a small stone stool, cradling a Pokémon egg in its arms. It took out a handkerchief and carefully wiped the egg, emitting a gentle warmth from its body, like a living heater.

Tinkaton sat beside it, occasionally pulling out a small piece of metal from somewhere and comparing it to Charcadet's body, seemingly measuring for something.

"Ku~zzZ"

Kubfu sat in front of the television, dozing off as the warmth lulled it into sleep.

Johan, meanwhile, was in the kitchen with the Indeedee siblings and Alcremie.

From the occasional sounds and the delicious aroma wafting out, it was clear that something tasty was being prepared.

Outside the window, snow mixed with rain drizzled down, casting a misty veil over the streets.

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar peeked its head in through the door, smoothly opening it to let Cynthia in.

"You're here?"

Hearing the movement, Johan poked his head out of the kitchen.

"Mm."

Cynthia's cheeks were slightly flushed. With Gengar's enthusiastic assistance, she took off her coat, revealing her slender, alluring silhouette.

Johan wanted to ask—whoever said Cynthia didn't have a great figure?

"The snow in Kanaz City doesn't feel cold at all."

She smiled.

"Hoenn is further south than Sinnoh, after all. If it weren't for the ocean, we probably wouldn't see snow at all."

Noticing the Garchomp and Lucario behind her, Johan naturally retracted his gaze.

Earlier, after capturing the Pokémon hunters, Johan had gone to the Kanaz Police Station to submit a brief report since he was the one who initiated the operation.

After that, he came home to start preparing dinner.

Meanwhile, Cynthia had gone out sightseeing in Kanaz City with Gengar as her guide.

According to Gengar, it wanted to show her the "empire" it had built!

Indeedee's sharp awareness of unfamiliar faces wasn't just due to its own vigilance. The many stray Pokémon that Gengar had "convinced" to help out in Kanaz City also played a big role.

The café provided free food for them, and in return, they were happy to help out with small tasks—like handing out flyers.

"Mm."

Cynthia nodded. "We went to the supermarket and bought some ingredients."

As she spoke, Gengar began pulling out bag after bag of groceries from its mouth.

"You two went to the supermarket?" Johan's eyes widened.

He glanced at Cynthia and Gengar.

A "chronic indecisive" shopper and a "never-thought-about-it" impulse buyer went to the supermarket together?

Watching as Gengar pulled out its seventh, eighth, and ninth large bag, Johan realized—

That purple chubby ghost had taken advantage of Cynthia to buy everything Johan usually refused or limited it from getting!

"Did you buy out the whole supermarket?" Johan couldn't help but ask.

Cynthia chuckled. "Gengar liked them."

"Ka-kaah~!"

Exactly!

Gengar floated behind Cynthia and started massaging her shoulders, looking smug, as if it had found a new "patron" and "financial backer."

"Besides," Cynthia added with a playful smile, blinking at him, "you're cooking for us anyway."

"Making… this much?" Johan felt his head ache.

"Garrr!!"

Garchomp, standing behind Cynthia with its arms crossed, let out a low growl and cast a cold, sidelong glance at him, flashing its sharp scythes.

You spent our money, so this better be good—otherwise, beware of my blades!

Though it sounded threatening, its gulping betrayed its true intentions.

Johan's cooking skills were well-known. Otherwise, Garchomp wouldn't have let Gengar and Cynthia buy so much food in the first place.

"Pretty please~?" Cynthia teased.

Who could resist that?

"Don't worry!" Johan straightened his back.

With a wave of his hand, he declared,

"Alright, everyone, move out!"

With so many ingredients and hungry mouths, plus the chilly weather…

Nothing would be more fitting than hotpot!

The group cheered and quickly got to work.

Even Kubfu, who had been dozing off, snapped awake when its snot bubble popped, dazed but instinctively joining the activity.

In the Garden…

"Ka-kaah~ Ka-kaah~"

Gengar and Dragonite stretched out a massive oilcloth over the open space, forming a makeshift tent. Supporting it were Garchomp, Roserade, Milotic, and Tinkaton.

Roserade and Milotic held up the cloth with poles, Garchomp straightened it, and Tinkaton secured it in place with stakes and rope.

Johan, the Indeedee siblings, Alcremie, and Rotom got busy setting up the table underneath.

"Ninetales, freeze these meats a little—it'll enhance the flavor."

He handed over trays of freshly sliced beef that Rotom had just prepared.

In his previous life, he'd heard of "ice hotpot," where chilling the beef with ice enhanced its taste.

The same trick worked for beef tripe, maw, and fresh shrimp.

"Oowoo~~"

Ninetales exhaled a chilling mist, swiftly forming a miniature "ice mountain" beside it.

Being an aesthetic perfectionist, it hummed a little tune while using its tail to neatly arrange the ingredients.

Honestly, with its plating skills, Ninetales could easily land a job at a high-end restaurant.

"Kubfu, these are for you—pound them into meatballs!"

"Ku~!"

Wielding two wooden clubs like an expert, Kubfu started hammering away.

Bam, bam, bam—

"Marsha, stop staring—devein these shrimp."

"Marsha?"

Marshadow, hiding under the table, blinked in confusion at the enormous bowl of shrimp suddenly placed in front of it.

A grand warrior of the shadows… you want me to devein shrimp?!

Marshadow fumed.

Then, in a fit of rage… it removed every single shrimp vein in seconds!

"Marsha!"

Hands on its hips, Marshadow huffed indignantly.

Hmph! You think I'm easy to bully?

Thud!

"Here's another bowl."

Marsha! |(▼皿▼)

UNFORGIVABLE!

In its rage… it finished another bowl in record time.

"Charcadet, I need two more pieces of charcoal."

"Goka~"

Charcadet immediately placed two freshly heated charcoal pieces into the massive copper hotpot.

This was a custom-made Tinkaton creation—quality guaranteed.

"The grill needs more charcoal too. Lucario, make sure to flip the skewers so they don't burn."

"Ruka!"

Wearing an apron, Lucario nodded solemnly.

It found itself pondering—

Since when did it start following Johan's orders so naturally?

Lucario shook its head.

No, no—this is just for our family and friends!

It convinced itself with that reasoning.

Meanwhile, Cynthia was squatting near a flowerbed, inspecting some oddly familiar black and white radishes.

"Did… did you grow these?"

Johan glanced over. "Oh, yeah. Pull a few—they'll add some flavor to the hotpot."

"You know these are linked to a legendary Pokémon, right?" Cynthia asked curiously.

"Eh, they're just radishes. Eat up." Johan waved it off nonchalantly.

Soon, everything was ready.

Johan clapped his hands.

"Alright—let's eat!"

"Ka-kaah!"

"Draaa!"

"Cha!"

"Garrr!"

A chorus of excited cries erupted as hungry Pokémon swarmed in.

Johan passed Cynthia a dish of dipping sauce.

"Better hurry—if you're too slow, Gengar and the others will eat it all."

Cynthia giggled, watching the lively scene.

A warm, lively home like this…

Maybe this was the perfect way to live.

# 389: This Is No Joke (BONUS)

January 11th, Early Morning.

Cynthia sat in Johan's lounge chair in the courtyard, legs crossed, flipping through the book in her hands with a focused expression.

This was something Johan had written—wait, no, copied.

After all, being a "literary copycat" was actually a rather lucrative profession.

Especially in the Pokémon world, where because of Pokémon, not only was the technological tree skewed, but so were the cultural and entertainment industries.

"Hmm…"

Reaching the end, Cynthia flipped back to the first page, as if confirming the title of the book.

"About… Being Sealed for 500 Years Despite Being Overpowered from the Start?"

Johan took the cup of coffee handed to him by the younger Gallade, petting the Ninetales curled in his lap. He took a small sip and asked with a smile:

"What do you think?"

Cynthia nodded seriously, "It's quite good. I think… if Iris knew about this, she'd be eager to discuss it with you."

Iris, Cynthia's close friend and a future member of the Unova Elite Four, was also a novelist.

Johan's face stiffened. "I don't know her."

Cynthia smiled knowingly and returned to the book's topic.

"But… it doesn't seem finished?"

Johan subtly exhaled in relief before chuckling, "Yeah, the full title should actually be 'About Being Sealed for 500 Years Despite Being Overpowered from the Start, Only to End Up Babysitting My Weakling Master While Gathering an Army on Our Travels.'"

"…Huh?"

Cynthia tilted her head.

That title…

It sounded like quite a story.

She gently rubbed her chin.

"What?"

Cynthia handed the book back to Johan, flashed a bright smile, and said two words:

"Update it."

Johan: "…"

"Ku~ Kukuku~! zzZ"

Cynthia's gaze shifted to Kubfu, who was in the courtyard.

"Kubfu is so hardworking! I saw it practicing punches last night, and now it's still at it."

Johan scratched his head. "Yeah… our little Kubfu is just like that, very diligent."

He didn't know how to explain this "sleep-punching" thing to Cynthia.

Dream-Drunken Kung Fu?

Cynthia nodded thoughtfully.

"No wonder Lucario said that when it comes to pure punching technique, it might not even be able to beat Kubfu."

Pop!

Kubfu's snot bubble suddenly burst. It plopped onto the ground, rubbed its eyes, let out a long yawn, and stood up.

After dusting off its tiny black tail, it looked at Johan, rubbed its stomach, and smiled.

There was only one meaning behind this look—

Hungry.

Johan sighed with amusement.

Another "downside" of training in its sleep—Kubfu got hungry faster than anyone else.

After all, it was constantly exerting itself 24/7.

"Oh right, the Sinnoh League has prepared your reward. The next time you visit Sinnoh, it'll be delivered to you." Cynthia mentioned.

"Reward?" Johan was momentarily confused. "For pacifying Regigigas?"

Why did they make it so complicated?

He had to wait until he visited Sinnoh to receive it?

"What is it?" Johan asked curiously.

"I don't know the exact details, but from what I saw, it should be something quite valuable."

Why make it so valuable?

Johan rubbed his temple.

Cynthia chuckled. "Oh, and the research grant from our last investigation has also been processed."

"…Wait."

Johan's eyes lit up.

That meant—

"I already had my grandmother wire it to your account."

Johan quickly pulled out his phone and checked.

Sure enough—

10 million Pokédollars deposited!

After deducting the 1 million Pokédollars he had overspent on New Year's gifts for his Pokémon, he still had 9 million left!

Most importantly—

This was a grant, meaning no taxes!

"Alright! I'm treating you to a feast today!" Johan declared, waving his hand.

He still had an unused voucher from that seven-star restaurant he won earlier—time to see if it worked in Kanaz City.

January 13th, Monday Morning, Clear Skies.

Cynthia's short vacation came to an end. She rode on Garchomp's back, preparing to fly straight back to Sinnoh.

Unlike Johan, who got long holiday breaks as a teacher, she still had numerous research projects and field studies to complete.

Flying on her own was much more convenient.

"Well then, I'll be waiting for you in Sinnoh."

Cynthia smiled down at Johan from atop Garchomp.

The Lily of the Valley Conference was starting soon. Johan had already registered with Professor Yamanashi.

"Alright!"

Cynthia gave a gentle smile and patted Garchomp's back.

"Gaaaarrr!!"

Garchomp let out a roar and shot into the sky, leaving behind two streaks of vapor trails.

Johan watched her silhouette shrink in the distance.

"Feels like… I'm forgetting something…"

He furrowed his brow.

"…Oh well."

It probably wasn't important.

Meanwhile, deep in the Fortree City forests.

Standing on the highest tree branch, Flint kept raising and shaking his phone, trying to get a better signal.

"What, just got a little wet and the signal's completely dead?"

Earlier, during their battle against the Pokémon hunters, he had gotten blasted by a Water Gun.

"Ku…"

Flint's Infernape held his ankles steady, growling softly.

Maybe we should've left with Lance and the others…

Plop—

Flint climbed down from Infernape's shoulders and confidently stood on the branch, clenching his fist.

"No worries! Johan and Clair will definitely come pick us up."

They had come to his rescue before—surely a little getting lost wouldn't be a big deal.

"Ku?"

Infernape scratched its head.

Are you sure?

They don't even know where we are…

Flint thumped his chest.

"They'll find us with this—"

A determined, battle-hungry heart!

Noon, Cold Weather.

Johan, in full lazy mode, lounged on his sofa, reading a book.

If the Pokémon egg hatched into a Ground-type, Giovanni's Secrets of the Earth would be extremely useful.

To be honest, he had read Secrets of the Earth multiple times, but every time, he found new insights.

It was no exaggeration—when it came to raising Ground-type Pokémon, Giovanni was among the world's best.

No wonder in Pokémon Adventures, Blue built a whole new team in just a year after receiving this book.

Ninetales curled up on Johan's chest, leaning back against him, reading along.

Johan was impressed with his Pokémon's literacy skills, but—

"You only learned Dig from this, why take it so seriously?"

"Oowoo~"

Turn the page already!

Johan smirked and decided to mess with it by pretending not to hear.

"Oowoo~!"

Ninetales puffed its cheeks and pawed at Johan's arm.

Seeing no response, it realized—this was on purpose.

"Oowoo~"

Irritated, it leaped onto his chest—

And started digging at him with its paws!

"Oof—"

Johan winced.

Ninetales froze, nervously glancing at him. Its deep blue eyes blinked twice.

Did… did it step too hard?

"I'm not soil for you to dig through." Johan chuckled.

"Oowoo~!"

Ninetales rolled its eyes—fooled again.

Johan pulled it into a tight hug and gave it a thorough petting until its fur puffed up, finally letting go with satisfaction.

No matter how much it grew, it was still adorable.

"Call incoming, Rotom!"

Rotom hovered over, carrying Johan's phone.

Caller ID—

Flannery?

Johan blinked.

"She should still be in Lavaridge Town for New Year's, right?"

Answering the call, he said:

"It's me."

Flannery, familiar as always, cut straight to the chase:

"Professor, I think something weird is happening at Mt. Chimney. Can you come over?"

"Mt. Chimney? Weird?"

Johan sat up straight.

"Flannery, this isn't a joke."

"Huh?"

In this world, Groudon's exact resting place was still uncertain.

The two most likely locations were either the Seafloor Cavern or Mt. Chimney.

Most scholars leaned toward Seafloor Cavern since Groudon, in its non-Primal form, was still part Ground-type, and it made little sense for it to rest in an underwater cave, which seemed more like Kyogre's domain.

But there was still a possibility—what if this world's Groudon actually preferred Mt. Chimney?

Just the thought of it made Johan feel a headache coming.

If that massive creature was really buried somewhere in Mt. Chimney…

"Professor, it's me, Steven."

At that moment, Steven's voice came through the phone.

Johan's eyebrows raised slightly.

Steven's at Mt. Chimney too?

He hadn't seen him in Fortree City recently—so he had been in Lavaridge digging for stones?

"I think this situation is similar to what we encountered last time," Steven continued.

Johan frowned. "The Camerupt incident?"

Back when they had last visited Mt. Chimney, they had encountered a berserk Camerupt. It had taken quite an effort to calm it down and capture it.

"Yes, something like that."

Berserk Pokémon had once been a frequent problem in Hoenn, keeping Johan busy for quite some time.

Lately, however, these incidents had subsided.

Now, out of nowhere, it was happening again?

"I understand."

Johan glanced at the glass window, where tiny water droplets had formed from the cold.

As long as this has nothing to do with Groudon, it should be fine.

Besides, Steven was there.

And the Lavaridge Gym Leader at this time was still Flannery's grandfather, the previous Fire-type Elite Four.

Though retired, his strength shouldn't have diminished too much.

"I'll get ready. I should be there before dinner."

Johan stood up, stretching slightly.

If nothing else, this could be a good training opportunity for his Pokémon.

Unfortunately, Cynthia had just left in the morning. Otherwise, the hot springs in Lavaridge Town would have been a nice bonus…

"Professor, the plane is already hovering over your house."

# 390: Looks Like You Need a Beating! (BONUS)

Hoenn, Lavaridge Town.

The moment one arrived near Mt. Chimney, a wave of heat rushed forward, instantly dispelling most of the winter chill.

The hot springs in Lavaridge Town were famous throughout the Pokémon world.

Unlike other locations where hot springs typically formed around extinct volcanoes, the ones in Lavaridge were directly linked to an active volcano, supposedly enriched with highly active minerals.

As soon as Johan arrived, he bought some local snacks for his Pokémon.

There was no choice—he hadn't been here in a while, and his Pokémon immediately locked onto the food stalls.

However, as Johan gazed at Mt. Chimney from a distance…

Nothing seems wrong?

It was still spewing volcanic ash. Although the sight of dark clouds blanketing the sky might look intimidating, in reality, there was no significant danger.

In fact—

Not only was it not hazardous, but these ashes nourished the forests at the mountain's base, enriching the soil and making the land more fertile.

Fallarbor Town was one of the biggest beneficiaries of this volcanic ash.

Some places even actively collected the ash, using it to craft various tools and glass products, such as the Glass Workshop in Hoenn.

They specialized in using volcanic ash to create unique items like glass flutes.

In a way, this was similar to Mt. Coronet in Sinnoh.

Except, instead of falling snow, Mt. Chimney rained down ash.

"Yeah, if there was an obvious problem with Mt. Chimney, the ones showing up here wouldn't be me but the entire Hoenn League's leadership…" Johan mused.

Mt. Chimney was too important for the Hoenn region.

If it ever posed a real crisis, it wouldn't just be a minor issue—it would be a regional emergency.

"Professor!"

Soon, Steven and Flannery arrived.

The three of them sat down in a dessert shop, where Johan listened to their report.

Flannery often wandered around Mt. Chimney in her free time—it was one of her hobbies.

Meanwhile, Steven had climbed to unreachable parts of the volcano in search of rare stones.

Then, the two of them unexpectedly ran into each other and noticed something strange happening.

Some of the wild Pokémon had abandoned their usual habitats.

At first glance, this might not seem significant.

However, in the wild, Pokémon were far more sensitive to environmental shifts than humans.

They wouldn't leave their territory unless something forced them to.

But that wasn't all.

Some of the wild Pokémon had attacked them.

These Pokémon weren't as crazed as the rampaging Camerupt from last time, but they were noticeably on edge and aggressive.

That was what made them think that something was wrong inside Mt. Chimney.

After hearing their account, Johan immediately made a decision.

"We're going now?" Flannery asked, her eyes lighting up.

Wait, why are you so excited?

Johan folded his arms, thinking for a moment before saying,

"Mt. Chimney is important. If something's really wrong, we need to report it to the League ASAP."

"Professor is right," Steven agreed.

"So what's the plan?" Flannery asked. "We'll probably run into aggressive Pokémon again, maybe even something big."

Johan had already thought of that.

"You're best at handling Fire-types. You take the lead."

"Me?"

Flannery blinked, then nodded. "Makes sense."

Her Rapidash had Flash Fire, which made it ideal for dealing with sudden Fire-type attacks.

Seeing Flannery accept her role so quickly, Johan's mind drifted to another person.

If that guy was here, things might be even easier…

Oh well.

At worst, they still had Steven's ultimate Metagross as backup.

"Let's go."

Hoenn Region, Mt. Chimney

The sun had begun to set, but Mt. Chimney still burned with a crimson glow.

Occasionally, flames flickered from cracks in the mountainside, accompanied by dark plumes of smoke.

"…Was the lava inside this active before?" Johan asked.

He distinctly remembered that the cracks didn't spit flames like this last time.

"The lava in Mt. Chimney pulsates periodically," Flannery explained, frowning. "We're currently in an active phase, but… this seems excessive."

Just because a Pokémon lived in a volcano didn't mean it enjoyed lava.

Some Pokémon liked the heat, while others only liked the minerals in the volcanic rock.

Steven narrowed his eyes.

"So you're saying the problem might be inside the volcano?"

He and Johan exchanged glances.

Both of them thought of the same Pokémon.

The legendary Pokémon Heatran—Mt. Chimney's supposed guardian.

"I know a shortcut into the volcano!" Flannery said, jumping onto her Rapidash.

A true one-horsepower engine.

Technically, if Groudon really was buried here, then Heatran shouldn't be living in Mt. Chimney.

Even though Heatran was a "mass-produced" legendary, it was still a legendary Pokémon.

Who would want to live next to Groudon?

Even Fire-types needed fire resistance, after all.

"Stay alert," Johan warned.

Better to be cautious.

"Rotom, start scanning."

"Understood, Roto!"

Inside Mt. Chimney

A wave of heat washed over them.

They walked along the cracked lava paths, their world bathed in red light.

Lava bubbled beside them, forming thick, boiling-hot gas bubbles that burst into steam.

Johan ordered Gengar to stay inside his shadow, while Rotom floated nearby, absorbing excess heat.

The temperature suddenly felt a lot more bearable.

Meanwhile, Flannery and Steven were already soaked in sweat.

Ghost-types really were fantastic.

"Professor!" Flannery took a deep breath and wiped her forehead. "You were right. The lava levels shouldn't be this high—this path used to be wider."

Molten rock had flooded most of the area, forcing them onto an even narrower path.

This was important information.

"Also, I haven't seen a single Slugma or Magcargo," Steven added, his expression grim.

Johan understood immediately.

He closed his eyes and activated Aura.

A wave of perception spread outward, mapping out the hidden parts of the volcano.

BOOM!

The lava churned violently, chunks of rock tumbling into the magma below.

A deep rumbling echoed from the volcano's core.

Johan's eyes snapped open.

"…RUN!"

Flannery and Steven didn't hesitate—they bolted.

Right behind them, a tidal wave of lava surged forward, devouring the narrow path.

If they had been a second slower, they would have been swallowed alive.

The entire cave trembled, rocks crashing down from above.

"Metagross!"

"Kubfu!"

Steven and Johan acted instantly.

Two Poké Balls burst open.

Shiny Metagross swung its mighty steel arms, smashing the falling rocks into dust.

Meanwhile, Kubfu punched straight through the boulders blocking their path, clearing the way forward.

ROOOOAR!!

A terrifying roar echoed behind them.

A monstrous figure emerged from the lava, standing atop the molten waves like a king of fire.

As expected—Heatran!

Seeing it in person, Johan felt his pulse quicken.

Last time, the Heatran they met was friendly.

But this one?

This one looked like it wanted to kill them.

Johan could feel Heatran's rage through his Aura.

"That thing's huge! What is it?!" Flannery gasped.

A roar from Heatran shook the entire cavern.

"You two, keep clearing the way," Johan said calmly.

"I'll try to reason with it."

He extended his Aura again.

"Heatran!"

…

The response?

"DENIED!"

Johan's eye twitched.

"…Looks like you need a beating."

# 391: Johan vs. Heatran! (BONUS)

With Kubfu leading the way, Johan and his team safely returned to the entrance.

However, that didn't mean they were out of danger.

Between the entrance and the other side of the mountain lay a long and winding tunnel.

The massive commotion caused by Heatran could easily lead to a tunnel collapse if they weren't careful.

Additionally, the magma within the cave was unusually turbulent. If they recklessly charged into the tunnel to escape, the backflow of magma could prove just as troublesome and dangerous.

"We can't let Heatran get any closer. Its movements could bury the tunnel!"

Steven, with his experience in mining, spoke with authority.

As he spoke, he threw out two Poké Balls.

"Aggron, Armaldo, the tunnel is in your hands!"

"Garrr~!"

"Ka-kah~!"

Clad in heavy metallic armor, Aggron possessed the sheer strength to withstand any boulders that might fall within the tunnel.

Likewise, the armored Armaldo could use its sharp claws to ensure the tunnel remained unobstructed.

"Ninetales, go help them," Johan said.

"Oowoo~"

Ninetales leaped from his belt.

Despite the cave's scorching heat and the surging magma, Ninetales wasn't entirely powerless—though it was certainly less agile than it would be outside.

Instead of fighting, it was better suited to reinforcing the tunnel alongside Aggron and Armaldo by freezing and strengthening it.

The arrival of the nimble Ninetales brought a refreshing chill behind Johan's team, slightly alleviating the oppressive heat from the magma.

"Garrr!!"

Heatran paid no heed to Johan's plans and let out a deafening roar.

A scorching, golden-red light rapidly gathered in its mouth.

"Teacher!"

Flannery, who had been holding back the magma, shouted, signaling him to look at Heatran.

"I see it!" Johan responded gravely.

Within his sight, information about Heatran's incoming move materialized.

Magma Storm!

Heatran's signature move!

Not only did it possess immense destructive power, but it also created a prolonged fiery storm that prevented Pokémon from switching out.

Given the current terrain and magma-filled environment, the move would likely bypass accuracy checks altogether.

"Flannery, go find the Volcanic Keystone! Steven and I will handle this!" Johan ordered, frowning.

"The Volcanic Keystone?"

Flannery was momentarily stunned.

"It's a solidified rock formed from cooled magma, closely linked to Heatran's existence. I suspect the keystone has loosened, which is why Heatran is so agitated."

The exact relationship between the Volcanic Keystone and Heatran was unclear.

To draw a comparison, it was akin to the connection between Spiritomb and the Odd Keystone.

In Pokémon Generations, Flint's younger brother, Michael, had calmed a raging Heatran by restoring the Volcanic Keystone to its rightful place.

Flannery didn't have time to question how Johan knew so much about Heatran.

The real issue was—how was she supposed to find the Volcanic Keystone in such a vast, magma-filled area like Mt. Chimney?

"Where do I look?" Flannery asked urgently.

Johan's mind raced.

He recalled his first visit to Mt. Chimney's interior with Steven, when their Gastly had discovered a peculiar stone within the mountain.

Perhaps that stone was the Volcanic Keystone?

"I think I know a place. Gengar!"

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar floated out from Johan's shadow.

"You remember, right?" Johan asked.

"Ka-kaah!" Gengar gave a thumbs-up—it knew exactly what he was referring to.

"Great!" Johan turned to Flannery. "Gengar will lead you there!"

"Understood!"

Flannery quickly summoned her Torkoal, which dove into the magma. She leaped onto its back.

With Gengar guiding them, Flannery and Torkoal set off in search of the Volcanic Keystone.

But first, they had to get past Heatran!

"Garrr!!"

At that moment, Heatran's Magma Storm had fully charged, and a blazing torrent of molten fire erupted.

"Metagross!"

"Kon-ginn!!"

A layer of heavy psychic energy enveloped Steven's shiny Metagross as it charged straight toward the fiery onslaught.

Zen Headbutt!

BOOM!!

The terrifying collision sent molten lava flying. Metagross was blasted backward, crashing into the rocky wall.

To be fair—

Steven's Metagross was incredibly powerful, but against the Fire-Steel type Heatran, it was at an absolute disadvantage.

Moreover, the lava cave was Heatran's home turf, amplifying its strength the more the magma surged. Metagross alone wouldn't be enough to stop it.

Steven frowned and took a deep breath, reaching for his lapel pin.

Johan's eyes twitched—he quickly stopped him.

"Steven!" Johan called out. "I'll handle this."

Now wasn't the time for Mega Evolution.

Sure, Mega Metagross would gain a significant power boost.

But unless they could defeat Heatran in one decisive strike, that temporary surge in strength would only allow them to stall for a short while.

Besides, Steven wasn't yet fully proficient with Mega Evolution—if he overexerted himself here…

That would only increase Johan's burden.

Steven was briefly stunned, then relaxed.

Right.

Johan was here.

It wasn't time to go all out just yet.

Steven smiled slightly. "Teacher, I'll leave it to you."

Johan tossed out two Poké Balls.

"Dragonite, Charcadet!"

"Draaa~"

"Goka!"

Two figures, one large and one small, materialized.

Johan chose not to summon Tinkaton for now—like Metagross, it was a Steel-type and wouldn't fare well in this environment.

Thinking about it—

The magma wasn't just a problem for Steven. It hindered Johan too, albeit to a lesser extent.

"Dragonite, Water Pulse!"

While Flannery and Gengar had successfully drawn Heatran's attention, it sank lower into the magma, stirring it up further.

Whoosh—

A rushing stream of water struck Heatran, cooling its temperature and pulling its focus onto Dragonite.

"Garrr!!" Heatran's rage wasn't extinguished—it only grew stronger.

"Goka!"

Charcadet stepped forward.

"Sir, would you consider granting me some respect?"

Heatran didn't even glance at it.

"Goka! o(>O<;;)σ"

Charcadet turned to Johan, pointing at Heatran.

It's ignoring me completely!

Johan, who could relate, spoke with conviction.

"No need for words. Attack."

"Goka!"

A fierce flame erupted around Charcadet, forming a heat wave that rivaled Heatran's own.

"Ku~!"

Kubfu stepped up beside Charcadet, assuming a battle stance.

It had been training for so long, yet every time a major battle happened, it was stuck playing a support role.

Sure, "Coach Kubfu" was satisfied with its leadership role…

But!

Sometimes, it wanted to fight head-on too!

That was the pride of a legendary Pokémon!

Their combined auras flared, battle spirits soaring!

Johan, sensing their determination, smiled.

Very well.

They had already caught Heatran's attention.

Now all they had to do was hold it off.

"Dragonite, cover them."

"Draaa~"

Dragonite flapped its wings, staying beside Johan while pumping its fists in support of Charcadet and Kubfu.

It wanted to fight the powerful Heatran itself—but supporting its teammates was more important.

"Teacher…" Steven, still worried, stood beside Johan with Metagross. "Will they be okay?"

After all, Charcadet and Kubfu hadn't even evolved yet…

"They'll be fine!"

Johan's reply was firm, without a shred of hesitation.

If a Trainer didn't believe in their Pokémon—who would?

No matter the outcome, at this moment, they were undeniable.

"It's coming!"

"Garrr!!"

Heatran's golden-red energy surged once more.

Kubfu and Charcadet crouched slightly, ready to meet the attack head-on.

BOOM!

Flames exploded, shaking the entire cave violently—

The battle had begun.

The fiery shockwave swept through the cave, causing intense tremors as the burning magma churned wildly.

"Goka!!"

Charcadet roared, standing firm in the face of Heatran's inferno. Its small body trembled under the pressure, but its eyes remained unwavering.

Respect isn't given—it's earned!

If it wanted to be acknowledged, it had to prove itself first!

"Ku!!"

Behind it, Kubfu clenched its fists.

Even as its fur became singed by the heat, it didn't take a single step back.

One punch.

Then another.

And another.

Each strike tore through the raging flames, breaking small gaps in the fiery onslaught—only for the gaps to immediately close.

But Kubfu didn't stop.

Faster.

Stronger.

More precise.

Each punch flowed like a ceaseless tide, each strike more powerful than the last.

Heatran's eyes narrowed slightly.

This little one…

For a brief moment, a sliver of surprise flashed in its molten gaze.

But it didn't hold back.

Heatran let out another earth-shaking roar, gathering even more fiery energy in its core.

At that moment, Johan closed his eyes.

Aura.

Through the fusion of their energies, he traced the weaknesses in Heatran's attack—weaknesses that Kubfu had already begun exposing.

Johan's eyes snapped open.

"Found it!"

"Charcadet—Blast Burn!"

"Goka!!"

Flames exploded around Charcadet, surging in intensity as Johan infused his aura into the attack. The fire rippled with an unfamiliar energy, causing Heatran to instinctively flinch.

BOOM!!

A massive eruption engulfed the battlefield.

Johan's timing was perfect—right as Heatran's previous burst of energy had weakened and before it could unleash another attack.

The clash of heat waves sent ripples through the air, distorting the cavern's walls as magma surged violently.

Johan clenched his fist.

"It's our turn!"

"Kubfu—Close Combat!"

"Ku!!"

Kubfu took a deep breath, the heat distorting the air around it. Then, in the blink of an eye, it dashed forward.

Steven immediately reacted.

"Metagross!"

With a burst of psychic power, Metagross manipulated the shattered rock fragments, creating a path over the lava.

Kubfu raced forward, each stone beneath its feet vanishing the moment it passed.

Heatran's head snapped up—

And all it saw was a blur.

BAM!

A single, direct punch to its metallic body.

Followed by another.

And another.

And another.

Each strike landed faster than the last, relentless and overwhelming.

"Garrr!!"

Heatran let out a pained roar as the relentless barrage of blows forced it backward.

"Did we… win?"

Steven, watching the impossible unfold, murmured in disbelief.

Johan shot him a sideways glance.

Did the heat melt his brain?

Two-times effectiveness or not, Kubfu wasn't going to take down Heatran in a single round of attacks.

But still…

For Johan, Charcadet, and Kubfu—this was already a kind of victory.

No matter the damage dealt—

They landed their hits.

And that alone was enough.

"Ku! zzZ…"

Kubfu staggered backward, exhausted, but Metagross swiftly caught it.

Charcadet's flames flickered, its strength dimming.

But both Pokémon stood their ground.

Their eyes remained locked onto Heatran.

Heatran, on the other hand, stared at them—

And for the first time…

It acknowledged them.

Because pain was something it rarely experienced.

And now—

It had a fresh, burning wound on its back.

That charcoal Pokémon's flames…

That little bear's fists…

Both of them… had something real.

"Garrr!!"

Heatran let out a deep, rumbling growl—one of genuine battle spirit.

It had accepted them.

But it wasn't done fighting.

"Dragonite."

"Metagross."

Johan and Steven spoke simultaneously.

Their two strongest Pokémon stepped forward, prepared for the next round.

Just then—

HUMMMMM—

A mysterious glow shone from the depths of the magma, illuminating the entire cavern.

"Garrr…?"

Heatran turned its head.

The glow…

The Volcanic Keystone.

Someone had found it.

Johan and Steven exchanged glances.

Flannery was faster than expected.

# 392: Filing an Expense Report Isn’t Too Much to Ask, Right? (BONUS)

Johan signaled Steven to chase after Heatran while he immediately tended to Kubfu and Charcadet's injuries.

Though they had managed to withstand Heatran's attack and even launched a somewhat effective counteroffensive, they hadn't escaped unscathed.

The two little ones had suddenly become so determined—no doubt influenced by Dragonite, Gengar, and the others.

After all, while they were all close companions, no one wanted to be the one dragging the team down.

Especially after Dragonair evolved into Dragonite and saw a massive boost in strength.

Now, whenever these two spotted an opportunity for "experience," they got eager—let alone when that "experience" came in the form of a huge Heatran.

However…

Johan had a feeling that Charcadet's evolution wasn't entirely up to itself, whereas Kubfu's evolution might not be far off.

"Maybe… we should have a little in-team tournament?"

Not necessarily to determine rankings or victories—

But weren't those all just huge experience bags?

Why let those benefits go to outsiders?

"You two…"

Johan placed one hand on Charcadet's head and grabbed Kubfu by the scruff of its neck, lifting it up. He took a deep breath and smiled.

"Well done!"

"Goka!"

Charcadet's expression turned serious.

Maybe this time it had earned a bit of respect?

"Ku~zzZ"

Kubfu flailed its legs in the air, waving its fists.

I am Coach Kubfu! Do I not deserve some dignity?

Can't you wait until we're home to pick me up like this?

Seeing that they were in decent shape, Johan finally relaxed.

He called for Ninetales and Dragonite, then hurried in the direction Steven had gone.

Before long, they arrived at a chamber—one where Gastly had once accidentally ventured.

The magma within had now settled, and Heatran had also calmed down. Upon seeing Johan approach, Heatran seemed to recall—

Hadn't it been fed Pokéblocks before?

Its gaze then shifted to Charcadet and Kubfu.

It gave a slight nod.

"Garrr—"

These two were worth acknowledging.

But… their strength was still lacking compared to its own.

Suddenly—

Heatran felt a chill down its spine.

"Ka-kaah~"

Without it even noticing, Gengar had appeared behind it, one arm "friendly" draped over its shoulder.

Heatran turned its head—

Only to be met with Gengar's wicked, unsettling grin.

Long time no see, huh?

HUMMMM—

A Poké Ball on Johan's belt opened, and at long last, the long-suppressed Tinkaton emerged.

THUD!!

Its heavy hammer landed effortlessly on the ground, forming a deep indentation while causing the entire cave to tremble and loose rocks to fall.

Tinkaton's eyes gleamed.

"Cha~!"

You're a Steel-type, right?

BANG—

Dragonite landed on Heatran's other side, its pudgy arm casually resting on Heatran's opposite shoulder. It gave a few "gentle" pats.

Thud, thud—

Heatran staggered a few steps, nearly losing its footing.

"Draaa~?"

You nearly scared me back there. Turns out, you're one of "us"?

WHOOSH—

A frigid gust swept through the once-scorching magma cave. Frost crystals began forming under Heatran's feet, slowly creeping upward, threatening to encase it completely.

Ninetales landed lightly, turning its head toward Johan.

"Oowoo?"

Are you sure this thing is actually calm now? It seems much safer to just freeze it.

A bead of cold sweat rolled down Heatran's forehead.

How had it not noticed before—

This bunch was trouble.

If Johan had known Heatran's thoughts, he would've explained patiently:

First, its rage had indeed enhanced its strength while dulling its sense of pain.

Second, as a Pokémon residing in volcanic magma, Heatran's strength was tied to the activity of the volcano—explosiveness, terrain advantage, and so on.

Third, Johan's team hadn't just been worried about their own safety but also whether Heatran would "flip the table" and blow up Mt. Chimney.

And most importantly—

Just in the brief moment they had been "greeting" Heatran:

Gengar had looked at the Volcanic Keystone eight times, Ninetales three times, Dragonite five times, Kubfu five times, Charcadet eleven times, and Tinkaton… thirty-nine times!

Forty. Forty-one. Forty-two…

Alright, enough!

"Garrr—"

Heatran forced what it believed was a friendly smile.

Who would've thought that after all this time, Gengar still remembered exactly where the Volcanic Keystone was and had led Flannery straight to it?

When Johan arrived, Steven and Flannery both let out a small breath of relief.

Aura exists within the heart!

Now that Heatran seemed open to communication, Johan immediately used his Aura powers again.

This time, he quickly received a response from Heatran.

Huh?

Just as Johan had suspected—the magma's excessive activity had been caused by someone.

The resulting turbulence had dislodged the Volcanic Keystone, enraging Heatran.

The increase in magma activity had also enhanced Heatran's strength.

So, who was responsible for stirring up the magma in Mt. Chimney?

And for what purpose?

There were really only three possible culprits:

Team MagmaTeam AquaPokémon Hunters

Setting aside Team Magma and Team Aqua for now—

If Pokémon Hunters were involved, they were likely trying to create chaos to either escape or take advantage of the situation.

But Johan had a strong feeling it was either Team Magma or Team Aqua.

"Are they finally getting restless… or are they searching for the Crimson Orb?"

Meanwhile, in a secret base far from Mt. Chimney—

"Boss, the volcanic activity at Mt. Chimney has subsided. Looks like someone noticed and intervened."

A young man in a red robe sat with his legs crossed at a desk, studying data on his computer with a grin.

Standing beside him, a bespectacled middle-aged man—arms crossed in a classic commanding posture—remained unfazed. He simply muttered:

"Just a tiny piece of meteorite… and with the right device, it triggered such a drastic reaction…"

If Johan were here, he'd instantly recognize them—Team Magma!

The bespectacled man was Maxie, the leader of Team Magma!

And the one seated below him—one of the "Three Flames" of Team Magma—Tabitha.

Hearing Maxie's words, Tabitha smirked. "Shame that Greater Mauville Holdings was only willing to hand over a tiny fragment of that meteorite."

Maxie adjusted his glasses subtly.

"The fact that they were willing to part with one means they likely have at least one more—if not more."

Tabitha grinned, eyes flashing with interest.

"Boss, should we—"

Maxie shot him a calm glance.

Tabitha immediately shut up.

"The time isn't right yet."

"Yes, sir."

Back in the present—

"Meteorites?"

Steven looked puzzled. He didn't understand why Johan had suddenly asked him to pay attention to meteorites and fossils in the market.

"Heatran said it saw a small meteorite fall into the magma—and that's when everything became unstable," Johan said smoothly.

Heatran blinked.

Did I say that?

Catching Johan's meaningful look, Heatran quickly nodded.

Yes! Yes, I said that!

Johan smiled. Gengar immediately understood and, though reluctant, pulled out a box of Pokéblocks and patted Heatran's back.

"Ka-kaah~"

We're friends now.

Heatran eyed the Pokéblocks and swallowed.

It glanced around.

Friends, huh?

Better than being enemies!

Taking the Pokéblocks in its mouth, Heatran slipped back into the Volcanic Keystone.

Johan let it be—his mind was elsewhere.

In the original story, Team Magma had used a device to amplify meteorite energy to agitate Mt. Chimney's magma in an attempt to expand landmass.

Honestly… Johan thought it was a pretty dumb plan.

But sometimes, there was no reasoning with fools—what they saw as a grand cause was a catastrophic mistake to everyone else.

And when it came to meteorites, Steven knew far more than he did.

"I understand. I'll look into it," Steven said seriously.

The Hoenn region… was far more unstable than it seemed.

It was time to start preparing some backup forces.

At Lavaridge Town's Pokémon Center—

"Johan, your Pokémon have fully recovered. They're in great condition," Nurse Joy said with a warm smile as she handed the Poké Balls back to Johan and his companions.

"Thank you, I appreciate it," Johan replied with a grin.

"It's my duty," Nurse Joy responded, her cheeks slightly flushed.

Johan glanced down at the Poké Balls.

Kubfu had already fallen asleep—likely exhausted. For once, it hadn't continued training even in its sleep.

As for Charcadet…

Johan had sensed something different about it during their battle.

Through his Aura, he had felt Charcadet's transformation.

Just like how charcoal burns brighter over time, Charcadet's "life fire" seemed to have been ignited by Heatran's Magma Storm. It was now burning even stronger.

The downside?

The chunks of charcoal it had been consuming had been completely burned away during the battle.

Looks like I need to restock on charcoal…

Wait.

Would this count as a workplace injury?

Filing an expense report for this isn't unreasonable, right?

Should he send it to the Investigation Bureau? Or maybe the International Police?

Actually… why not send it to both?

Whichever one approves it first, I'll take it. If both approve it, even better.

"Alright, let's go soak in the hot springs," Johan announced, feeling quite pleased with himself.

Since they had made the trip to Lavaridge Town, it would be a shame not to enjoy the famous hot springs.

The last time he had soaked in one was back in Sinnoh.

That time, someone had ended up looking like a drenched Pidgey…

Huh?

Just as he stepped out of the Pokémon Center, Johan suddenly had a realization.

Wait a minute…

Where's Flint?

"What's wrong, Johan?" Steven and Flannery asked as they noticed him pause.

Johan coughed lightly. "Nothing."

Flint, a future Elite Four member, should be able to make it home on his own, right?

I'll just check in on him after the hot springs…

# 393: Let’s Play a Game of Cards!

Kanto, Saffron City, Rocket Media Entertainment Corporation Headquarters.

Inside a somewhat inconspicuous, secluded office—

"Please, have a seat."

Matori, dressed in a sharp tailored suit, a fitted pencil skirt, black stockings, and high heels, adjusted her glasses slightly and gestured to the figure in front of her.

The person wore a red cloak and had long, wavy, light-blue hair. As they sat down, a glimpse of a Poké Ball was revealed beneath their cloak.

"Matori, a pleasure to meet you," the man said flatly.

"You…" Matori was momentarily at a loss for words upon seeing the man before her. After a pause, she finally continued, "You said you came to discuss a collaboration?"

"Indeed." The man ran a hand over his waist—not to retrieve a Poké Ball, but a deck of… cards?

"A card player…?"

"Hm?"

"No, I mean, what are those cards?"

"I am Darkrai," the man introduced himself, his tone steady. "I am dedicated to developing Pokémon Trading Cards. I heard that your company is currently seeking partners for Pokémon-related merchandise, so I came to discuss an opportunity."

"Pokémon Trading Cards?"

The moment Darkrai mentioned Pokémon TCG, he became noticeably more talkative.

His words were filled with admiration for the game, leaving Matori completely bewildered.

Phrases like "Resilient, Invincible, Supreme, Unstoppable, Ultimate Cheer!" and "The bond between Trainer and Cards"—

A bond… with cards?

"Uh, excuse me for interrupting—Mr. Darkrai, you're a Trainer?" Matori attempted to steer the conversation back to a topic she could comprehend.

"Mhm," Darkrai responded calmly.

Then, with renewed enthusiasm, he added, "Matori, let's play a round of cards! You'll soon understand its true charm!"

Matori opened her mouth but hesitated before saying, "I… I don't have any…"

"No problem!"

Darkrai pulled out another deck. "I have spares. You can borrow one."

He placed the second deck in front of her and—

"I'll teach you."

Matori: "…"

"Darkrai?"

Johan, upon receiving Matori's call, fell into deep thought.

Wasn't that the 'Legendary Pokémon Guy' from Sinnoh's Lily of the Valley Conference?

Rumor had it he was a character inspired by an actual Pokémon TCG Champion, and throughout the entire tournament, he had only used two Pokémon—both Legendary or Mythical.

"A Pokémon TCG Company President?"

Hearing Matori's description, Johan snapped back to reality.

"Wait."

He suddenly realized—

"What does Darkrai want with you? An advertisement?"

Wasn't Matori in charge of Rocket Media Entertainment Corporation?

Didn't they only produce TV series?

At the moment, they only had one show—'Meowth & Pichu'—though it had become wildly popular.

"A collaboration? Merchandising?"

Johan sank into thought again.

To be fair, merchandising was a brilliant idea.

Johan had seen firsthand the power of the merch industry—and he had to admit, Giovanni's business acumen was impressive. He had quickly realized the potential of using their TV series to drive merchandise sales.

"…Possible ties to League Chairman Damalanchi and the Berlitz Family from Sinnoh?"

Johan suddenly felt overwhelmed by the sheer amount of information Matori had just shared.

What does any of this have to do with anything?

They couldn't just assume Darkrai was connected to Chairman Damalanchi just because they both had "Dark" in their names, right?

Nor could they claim he was related to the Berlitz Family just because of a similar hair color.

That said, Pokémon TCG wasn't exactly uncommon in the Pokémon world.

For example, James from Team Rocket carried a Pokémon TCG deck around—but he used it as a Pokédex substitute.

"You handle it. You're the representative here."

After giving some brief instructions, Johan ended the call.

Darkrai or not…

Did he really have multiple Darkrai?

Since this was just a normal business negotiation, Johan didn't dwell on it.

At that moment, he was in a forest outside Fortree City.

No choice.

He had been unable to reach Flint, and everyone he asked said they hadn't seen him.

Eventually, Johan learned from Lance that—

After the mission ended, Flint had insisted on heading back alone.

Lance and Clair had left first, escorting the captured criminals.

"…I think I'm starting to understand Volkner."

Johan sighed softly.

If this was the kind of Four Heavenly Kings Flint was going to be… combined with the reckless Aaron… Volkner was going to have a lot on his plate.

"Draaa~~"

Above him, Dragonite's voice called out.

Johan's expression shifted.

Did they finally find him?

Moments later—

"Johan!!"

A disheveled, tattered Flint came running towards him, eyes slightly red.

"I knew you'd find me!"

He reached out, trying to grab Johan's hands—

Only for Johan to dodge effortlessly.

Johan gave him an unimpressed glance and said flatly, "Flint, you're famous now."

"Famous?"

Johan pulled out his phone and made a call.

"Hello, it's me—Johan. Yeah, I found that reckless fire-head kid. Thanks for all your hard work."

"…Who, who was that?"

After hanging up, Johan spread his hands and said, "Who else? The League Police from Fortree City, Fallarbor Town, Slateport City, and Mauville City."

Flint froze in place.

"…That many people?!"

"More importantly, I'm really curious—how did you get yourself lost? And why was your phone unreachable?"

Pokémon world's communication systems were top-notch—except in special ruins, hidden realms, or mysterious dimensions, there were hardly any dead zones.

The entire system was designed so that Trainers could always find their way.

Flint scratched his head awkwardly.

"…I only realized later that my phone had gotten waterlogged and broke."

"…Six."

Johan turned and started walking back.

"Johan, I heard that the Fleur-de-lis Institute in Kalos has developed a new holographic phone—think it's waterproof?" Flint quickly caught up.

"You have to understand—sometimes, a phone isn't just a phone."

"…What do you mean?"

"In certain situations—especially in wartime—its purpose isn't just communication." Johan brushed aside a branch.

"…Wartime? Hahaha, what, does it explode or something?"

Flint laughed—

Until he noticed Johan wasn't denying it.

"…Wait, does it actually explode?"

"I never said that."

Flint shuddered.

Quickly, he changed the subject.

"Johan, how did you find me?"

"Did you rely on our shared fighting spirit?"

"…Why aren't we taking Dragonite?"

Johan suddenly stopped and looked directly at Flint.

"If you say one more word—I'll leave you here forever."

Flint immediately clamped his mouth shut.

He wasn't usually this talkative—he had just been wandering in the forest alone for so long that he was dying for conversation.

January 15th, Clear Skies.

Hoenn, Fortree City, Pokémon Center.

"Hahaha—Flint is fully revived!"

Emerging from the Pokémon Center, now wearing a fresh set of clothes, Flint swaggered out, hands in his pockets.

At a nearby café, Johan—one hand petting Ninetales, the other holding a book—subtly adjusted his position, trying to pretend he didn't know him.

Unfortunately, Flint spotted him immediately.

"Johan! Let's have an intense battle!"

"…Fine."

Seeing no escape, Johan put down his book and agreed.

Flint's eyes lit up.

"Where's the battle arena?"

"No need."

Johan clenched his fist.

"We'll go to the wilds."

The wilds?

Flint scratched his head. "Alright! As long as we don't start a forest fire—"

Johan smirked slightly and added, "No restrictions."

Flint: "!!"

Johan was infamous for his hand-to-hand combat skills. A no-restrictions battle with him?!

"Hahaha—uh, haha, ahahaha—"

If I laugh long enough, I can avoid this fight, right?

"Let's go."

"W-where to?"

"You wanted a battle, didn't you? I'll introduce you to an opponent." Johan's lips curled into a sly smile.

"…Are they strong?" Flint suddenly got excited.

"Very strong." Johan's tone was absolute.

"Let's go! Let's go!"

Even if I can't beat Johan, surely I can beat someone else, right?

Flint had been stuck in that forest for far too long—he and his Pokémon were desperate for a fight.

Fortree City, Northern Shore.

WOOOOOO—

A deep ship horn echoed across the sea.

A massive wooden sailing vessel was docked at the shore. Johan and Flint made their way through the trees before arriving at the scene.

Standing at the bow of the ship was an elderly man with a white beard, a captain's hat, and an open naval coat. He stood with arms crossed, smiling at the two approaching figures.

Flint's steps stuttered.

"…Uh, Johan… Who is this…?"

Johan, however, kept his usual calm demeanor. Stepping onto the ship's wooden deck, he slightly bowed and greeted the old man—

"Drake."

"Hahahaha! Johan, my boy! Long time no see!" Drake's booming laughter carried across the sea.

It really had been a while.

After the Mt. Chimney incident, Johan had submitted his report to Drake—

One reason was to alert him about Team Magma's possible involvement.

The other reason?

To file for reimbursement.

Drake, in response, had told him that his ship was docked at Fortree City's northern shore and that Johan should swing by if he had the time.

And so—

Johan had come.

And brought a lost Flint with him.

"Drake, let me introduce you—" Johan turned to Flint.

"This is Flint, from Sinnoh. He's here to challenge you."

Flint's expression froze.

Wait.

The opponent Johan found for me is…

A FOUR ELITE?!

"Oh?"

Drake raised an eyebrow in interest. His gaze fell upon Flint, and after a moment, he let out a hearty chuckle.

"Well then! We still have some time before departure—this will be a nice warm-up!"

Johan casually leaned against the ship railing, his Pokémon all sitting beside him.

Then—

They all turned away at the same time.

They couldn't bear to watch.

# 394: Volkner Recommends It—A Trusted Choice!

"Johan."

Just as Johan was watching Flint's battle with Drake—oh no, his one-sided beating—a voice called out to him.

He turned toward the sound.

It was Bertha, the Sinnoh Elite Four member!

Huh?

Why was Bertha on Drake's ship?

Could it be…

Cough, cough.

"Bertha," Johan greeted her with a smile.

"That Pokémon egg…" Bertha's eyes fell on the egg cradled in Charcadet's arms. "A Ground-type Pokémon's egg?"

"You recognize it?" Johan raised an eyebrow in slight surprise.

"Not exactly, but I can tell it's related to a Ground-type Pokémon. Look at these patterns…"

Johan sighed internally.

The harder it is for others to recognize, the more certain I am about what's inside.

"If you need any guidance in raising a Ground-type Pokémon, feel free to ask me," Bertha said with a kind smile.

"Definitely."

Even though Johan had Giovanni's 'Secrets of the Earth', Bertha was still a Sinnoh Elite Four member. She must have her own expertise.

Bertha pursed her lips, pausing briefly before saying,

"Actually, the reason we called you here…"

Huh?

Johan was momentarily confused.

Wasn't it Drake who called me here?

Was Bertha involved as well?

"…is because we're forming the 'Sinnoh-Hoenn Joint Special Task Force to Eliminate Pokémon Hunters.'"

What a ridiculously long name.

"After discussions between me, Drake, and Anabel, we would like you to serve as the leader of this 'Special Task Force.'"

"Me?"

Johan pointed at himself.

Why?!

If I did something wrong, just tell me—I'll change!

Sure, Johan hated Pokémon Hunters. But he also understood one thing—

As long as the black market exists, as long as there's demand, these hunters will never fully disappear.

Bertha nodded. "Our main target is the Pokémon Hunter syndicate led by Hunter J."

Johan immediately understood.

So the goal is primarily Hunter J and her crew.

That made things much more straightforward.

Besides, Johan and Hunter J already had plenty of personal grudges.

Still, he decided to struggle a little.

"I'm not exactly an expert in this field… Wouldn't you or Drake be better suited for this role?"

Bertha smiled faintly. "Drake and I are too high-profile."

Her eyes narrowed slightly, a cold glint flashing through them.

"The last time we organized a major raid, we planned everything meticulously. But in the end, they got away at the last minute. You can probably guess why."

There was a mole inside the League!

Or, more precisely, Team Galactic, led by Cyrus, was covertly aiding Hunter J.

But Johan's identity was still 'clean.'

Moreover, just days ago, Johan had orchestrated a flawless operation to capture a team of Pokémon Hunters—not a single one had escaped.

"…So," Bertha continued, "you have both the experience and the ability for this job."

Johan rubbed his temples. "Well, I think…"

At first, he wanted to say Cynthia or Steven would be better candidates.

But after a second thought—

Cynthia was hard to drag into this.

Steven? He was his student—how could a teacher dump the responsibility onto his pupil?

Besides, Steven had just managed to pull himself out of the chaos between being a Trainer, Devon Corporation's heir, and a rock-collecting enthusiast. It wouldn't be fair to pile more on him.

So Johan said, "Volkner seems like a good choice."

Sorry, Volkner.

Volkner was smart, meticulous, and highly capable—he would definitely be a great fit for this role.

Bertha blinked.

"Volkner recommended you."

Johan: "???"

Excuse me?

Since when did Volkner have a seat at high-level League meetings?!

Has he climbed that high already?!

After Bertha's explanation, Johan finally understood.

It turned out that when Volkner had told him Bertha had asked him for a favor, this was what she meant.

But later, Volkner felt he was inexperienced and unsuited for the job—so he recommended Johan instead.

That's why Bertha proposed his name at the meeting.

"Okay, Volkner, I see how it is," Johan gritted his teeth internally.

"How about Anabel, then?" He refused to give up.

In terms of experience, authority, and rank, Anabel was far more qualified than him.

"Anabel would have been a perfect choice," Bertha admitted. "But recently, the International Police Countermeasures Division was established, and Anabel has been appointed as its Director."

Johan: "…"

So there's literally no one else?!

The Elite Four are too high-profile.

The Gym Leaders are also too conspicuous.

Among non-League members…

Flint? Aaron?

Good lord—there really weren't many reliable choices left.

Bertha smiled again.

"Don't worry. In critical moments, Drake, myself, and even others will follow your command."

Johan slowly raised his hand.

"I have one more question—as a Pokémon Academy teacher…"

As he spoke, Bertha also spoke, their voices nearly overlapping.

"And don't worry about funding—hm?"

"…A teacher should shoulder more responsibility! Pokémon Hunters are scum that must be eradicated!" Johan's face turned serious.

Bertha was deeply moved and said warmly,

"If only the League had more young Trainers like you, I could retire sooner."

"You're too kind, Bertha. You're not old at all!"

Bertha chuckled at his flattery.

"Right, you said you had one more question?"

Johan pondered briefly, then said,

"Since I'm a teacher, my main duty is education. I won't always be available…"

"Hm."

Bertha nodded. It was true—Johan couldn't abandon teaching entirely for this mission.

But it was precisely this sense of responsibility that made him even more trustworthy.

"So, you're asking for…?"

"A team." Johan didn't hesitate.

"Deal!" Bertha immediately agreed. "I see you're close with Flint and Aaron—they can assist you."

Johan: "???"

These two?!

Johan took a deep breath and said firmly, "Then I want one more person!"

"Who?"

"Volkner!" Johan said without hesitation.

Volkner and these two must be a package deal!

Otherwise, I'll turn into a full-time babysitter!

Johan fully acknowledged Volkner's abilities. With Volkner onboard, his workload would be significantly reduced—he might even be able to slack off a little.

And it's absolutely NOT because Volkner sold him out.

Nope.

I'm not being petty at all.

"No problem," Bertha agreed easily.

Volkner wasn't an Elite Four or Gym Leader, so he wasn't as high-profile.

And, to be fair—

Everyone who worked with Volkner had given rave reviews.

"A trusted choice."

Thus, in just a few exchanges—

The Sinnoh-Hoenn Joint Special Task Force to Eliminate Pokémon Hunters—shortened to the 'Special Task Force'—was officially established!

Leader: Johan.

Deputy Leader: Volkner.

Temporary Members: Flint, Aaron.

"By the way," Bertha suddenly remembered something. "Johan, you're still planning to participate in the Lily of the Valley Conference, right?"

"Will that bring too much attention?" Johan asked.

"No," Bertha shook her head with a smile. "I just wanted to confirm. After all, who would suspect that a conference participant is actually the leader of the Special Task Force?"

That made sense.

Bertha and Drake attracted attention because of their Elite Four status.

Johan nodded thoughtfully.

Still… something about this felt a little off.

Bertha's smile grew wider.

This was the perfect promotional opportunity.

ACHOO!

Sinnoh, Canalave City, Canalave Library.

Volkner, who had been leisurely sipping coffee while reading, suddenly sneezed.

In the silent library, it echoed loudly, drawing several irritated glares.

Volkner quickly gave an apologetic glance.

He rubbed his nose.

"That's weird…"

Did I catch a cold?

He stood up, carrying his book and coffee, and moved to a seat by the window. The warm sunlight streamed through, casting a golden glow over him.

He narrowed his eyes in comfort.

Perfect.

He had completed the task Bertha assigned to him, and now, with Johan taking over, he could return to a peaceful life.

Just thinking about it made him feel at ease.

"The world is such a beautiful place…"

"Oh, right."

Volkner pulled out his phone and swiftly blocked both Flint and Aaron.

Then, seeing Cynthia and Johan's contacts—

He shivered.

The consequences of blocking those two were too severe.

But this was enough.

He took a deep breath, exhaled slowly, and smiled.

"What a perfect afternoon…"

Drake's Ship.

"Here, your reimbursement package."

After finishing his battle—which left him feeling quite refreshed—Drake handed Johan a package.

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar opened its mouth, swallowed the package whole, and gave Drake an "OK" gesture.

Drake laughed heartily. He really liked both Gengar and Johan's straightforward personalities.

"But Johan," Drake said, patting him on the shoulder, "next time, tone down your reimbursement requests a little. Otherwise, I really won't be able to explain things to Boney."

Johan squinted at him.

To be fair, he had never bought a single piece of clothing for himself—

But wasn't it reasonable to claim compensation for a set of clothes ruined by magma?!

After some brief consideration, Johan nodded.

"90-10."

Drake's face became serious.

"70-30."

"80-20. Otherwise, I quit."

"Deal!"

After all, the Hoenn League had Devon Corporation as a tax-paying giant—in the Pokémon world, only the Kanto League could rival them financially.

"Alright, let's get you to Sinnoh!"

Drake, now in an even better mood, gave a hearty wave.

"Ka-kaah?!"

Gengar turned, startled by the sudden appearance of a familiar face.

Who was this?!

"It's me—Flint!"

Gengar shook its head furiously.

"Ka-kaah!"

Lies! Flint isn't this handsome!

# 395: Sweet Dreams and Nightmares!

January 15th, late afternoon. The sun was setting in the west, and a full moon gradually rose over the eastern sea.

Sinnoh, Canalave City.

Drake's ship had brought Johan and Flint to the closest Sinnoh port from Hoenn.

As for Bertha, she had other matters to attend to and did not stay. Instead, she left with Drake on the ship.

"So, Johan, that means you're officially my direct superior now?"

Flint seemed surprisingly calm about this revelation. In fact, there was even a hint of delight on his face.

Wouldn't this make challenging Johan even easier in the future?

"No."

Johan shook his head. "Technically speaking, you and Aaron report to Volkner."

"VOLKNER?!"

Flint's voice jumped eight octaves, and his eyes widened. "What did that guy do to deserve this?!"

Johan casually rubbed his ear.

"Because tonight, you're sleeping at his place."

"…Oh."

Canalave City, Volkner's Mansion.

"Johan, welcome~"

Volkner, who had already been informed of Johan's arrival, greeted him with a smile.

However, he clearly had not yet heard about his upcoming role as Deputy Leader of the Special Task Force.

"Hey, good evening, Volkner," Flint said casually, following behind Johan with his usual laid-back attitude.

"Who the hell are you?" Volkner glanced at him indifferently.

Flint was unfazed. He simply patted Volkner on the shoulder and strode inside as if he owned the place.

How could someone be this shameless?

And yet, Volkner knew—

There was nothing he could do about Flint.

Beat him up?

That would only play right into Flint's hands.

Besides, Volkner wasn't even completely sure he could win in a physical fight.

He sighed and led them both to the third-floor guest rooms.

Though called "guest rooms," they were fully equipped, spacious, and even more comfortable than most luxury hotels.

Since it was late, the three of them didn't do much aside from some light conversation before heading to their rooms for the night.

Outside, the night deepened.

A soft moonbeam cast its glow over the balcony.

Johan yawned and closed his laptop.

Now that he had accepted the Special Task Force position, it was time to start preparing properly.

He had been granted special access to the League's internal network, where he could review classified information unavailable to ordinary citizens.

"That's a beautiful full moon."

Gazing at the moon hanging high in the sky, Johan absentmindedly stroked Ninetales before heading to bed with his Pokémon.

"Speaking of which… Darkrai is supposed to be most active during the new moon, right?"

Near Canalave City, there were two islands—Fullmoon Island and Newmoon Island—which were home to Cresselia and Darkrai, respectively.

In the anime, it was mentioned that every year, Cresselia and Darkrai would visit Canalave City.

Darkrai would bring nightmares, while Cresselia would dispel them, granting people peaceful dreams.

"Logically speaking, there are twelve full moons a year. The chances of them showing up tonight are pretty low…"

Yawning again, Johan stretched.

It had been a long day—he had traveled quite a bit and wrapped up several important matters.

After freshening up, he climbed into bed, hugging Ninetales.

Tinkaton was already sprawled out across the mattress, snoring loudly.

Charcadet sat cross-legged with the Pokémon Egg in its arms, radiating a warm and comforting heat.

Dragonite had settled at the foot of the bed, smacking its lips in its sleep before finally settling down.

"Ka-kaah~"

Lights out.

"Gengar, the Ancient Greek God of Turning Off Lights" clicked the switch, plunging the room into darkness.

"Darkrai isn't even worthy to carry my flashlight," Johan muttered, half-asleep.

"Rotom, set an alarm for 10:00 AM."

"Got it, Roto!"

The Rotom Pokédex settled onto the nightstand.

Listening to Kubfu's steady breathing as it shadowboxed in its sleep, Johan's consciousness began to fade.

Midnight.

The full moon shone high in the sky, casting its gentle glow over the city.

Dark clouds, as soft as black velvet, slowly drifted in, obscuring the moon's light.

As the night deepened, Canalave City seemed to fall into an eerie stillness.

Then—

WHOOSH!

Johan suddenly sat up in bed.

At that exact moment—

From the ceiling, Gengar; from the bed, Ninetales and Tinkaton; from the foot of the bed, Dragonite; and from its meditative pose, Charcadet—

All of them opened their eyes at the same time.

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar drifted down from the ceiling, looking around suspiciously.

Something was trying to drag us into a nightmare?

But this…

This technique is so crude!

Not even worth comparing to Johan's Aura-based dream training—heck, even Gengar's own nightmare tricks were way better than this!

What kind of weak horror show was this? Just darkness and a few occasional screams? Lame.

"Oowoo?"

Ninetales yawned cutely.

"Should I tell a bedtime story so we can go back to sleep?"

But Gengar was already floating toward the balcony, full of curiosity.

Johan rubbed his temples.

Gengar wasn't wrong—the entity trying to drag them into a nightmare was shockingly bad at it.

Of course, it was also possible that…

Whoever was behind this was trying to pull in too many people at once, causing the technique to weaken.

To others, this might be a terrifying nightmare, but to Johan and his Pokémon…

It was nothing more than an annoying alarm clock going off in the middle of the night.

And really—

Who wouldn't be annoyed if their alarm woke them up at midnight?

"Ka-kaah!!"

"Hey, look! There are TWO moons in the sky!"

Gengar's voice echoed from the balcony.

The clouds had parted, revealing the original full moon—

And another figure, glowing in the moonlight like a rising crescent.

A majestic, ethereal form floated gracefully in the night sky.

Cresselia!

"Oowoo~"

"So beautiful~"

Ninetales and the others stepped onto the balcony, gazing at Cresselia in admiration.

Click!

The Rotom Pokédex immediately snapped a photo.

"Cresselia, the Legendary New Moon Pokémon! First-ever recorded image, Roto!" Rotom exclaimed excitedly.

In the Pokémon world, Cresselia's photos were incredibly rare.

After all, whenever it appeared, people were either asleep or already dreaming.

Johan followed Cresselia's line of sight—

And sure enough—

In the shadowy depths of a nearby forest, a black mist-like figure slowly began to rise.

A swirling mass of black and white mist, its hair-like wisps covering most of its face, long claws extending outward, while the shadows on its shoulders flickered ominously—

Darkrai!

"This night just keeps getting more interesting…"

Johan murmured.

Something feels off.

Just a few days ago, he encountered Heatran.

And now, on the same night, he was seeing Cresselia and Darkrai at once?!

Were Legendary Pokémon really this common now?!

A one-in-twelve chance, yet here it was…

…Wait.

Johan narrowed his eyes.

Was this Flint's fault?

Come to think of it, Flint did seem like a walking disaster magnet…

"Ka-kaah~!! (Д)σ"

"Hey, that Darkrai actually looks kinda cool!"

Gengar pointed at Darkrai, calling out excitedly.

Click!

"Darkrai, the Legendary Phantom Pokémon! Another rare find, Roto!"

As if noticing the noise, Darkrai turned toward them.

Its eyes narrowed.

Why… why aren't they affected by my nightmare energy?

Darkrai narrowed its eyes.

These people and their Pokémon… They weren't wearing Lunar Wings, so why weren't they affected by its nightmare energy?

A faint, nearly invisible ripple spread outward from Darkrai's body, washing over Johan and his team.

The next moment—

Darkrai froze.

Johan yawned, wiping away a tear at the corner of his eye.

Gengar scratched its backside, looking unimpressed.

Ninetales' gaze never left Cresselia, as if completely uninterested in Darkrai.

Dragonite chuckled, patting its belly in amusement.

Tinkaton was admiring its hammer in the moonlight, thinking it looked even shinier than usual.

Charcadet, on the other hand, was deep in thought, trying to figure out how to stop a fight if one broke out.

Did… Did it just fail?!

Darkrai was genuinely taken aback.

Was there something wrong with its abilities?

Just then—

From the next room over, a loud, bloodcurdling scream shattered the silence.

"AAAHHHHH!!! JOHAN!! CYNTHIA!! YOU THINK I'M SCARED OF YOU?! TONIGHT, I'LL FINALLY KICK YOUR ASSES! COME AT ME!!"

SLAM!

A vein twitched on Johan's forehead, but his expression remained calm and smiling.

Nope. No problem at all.

Darkrai, however, was utterly perplexed.

This… This had never happened before.

"Oowoo~~"

From above, Cresselia let out a soft, melodic cry.

Tiny, shimmering particles of light began to fall from the sky, scattering across Canalave City and pushing back the lingering darkness.

Seeing this, Darkrai turned its focus back to Cresselia.

There was no conversation—no exchange of words.

And yet—

In the blink of an eye—

BOOM!

The two collided midair, then quickly separated again.

A dark sphere formed in Darkrai's claws, absorbing all surrounding light, creating a void of pure darkness—

Dark Void!

Cresselia sang once more, radiant pink energy flowing around it. From the crescent moon behind its body, power gathered and condensed into a shimmering, rosy energy sphere—

Lunar Force!

BOOM!

The two powerful attacks clashed, illuminating the night sky.

"Ka-kaah! Ka-kaah?"

Gengar rubbed its hands together, watching with excitement.

"Why does that Dark Sphere look different from my Shadow Ball?"

"Oowoo~"

Ninetales gazed at Cresselia, mesmerized by the moonlight.

Soft, pink light began to gather around its body.
