# 396: Darkrai: Oh No, Am I Just a Substitute?

Cresselia and Darkrai are not unique Pokémon.

Johan knew of at least three Darkrai.

For example, the one owned by Tobias, the one right in front of him, and the one capable of holding its own against both Dialga and Palkia in a two-on-one battle.

That last Darkrai, the one that could stand against Dialga and Palkia, was most likely the strongest among them.

It might have even secretly learned Spacial Rend and Roar of Time.

After all, in the games, there was a special "Movie Darkrai" distributed during an event.

As for the Darkrai before him, while it wasn't weak by any means, Johan estimated it was roughly at the level of an Elite Four's main Pokémon.

So, while it posed a threat, it wasn't overwhelmingly dangerous.

"Oowoo~~"

The Ninetales was accumulating more and more lunar energy, its already snow-white fur now glistening under the full moon.

Hmm?

Noticing the change in Ninetales, Johan shifted his gaze toward it.

"Is this…?"

Moonblast?!

Now that he thought about it…

As an Ice/Fairy-type Pokémon, Ninetales had already demonstrated its prowess in the Ice-type domain to an exceptional degree—so much so that even Pryce couldn't help but praise it.

But that didn't mean its Fairy-type abilities were lacking.

It was just that, whether in Hoenn, Sinnoh, Kanto, or Johto, Fairy-type resources were relatively scarce.

Learning Fairy-type moves was much harder compared to other types.

More importantly…

From Johan's memory…

Alolan Vulpix and Alolan Ninetales could indeed learn Moonblast, but only through inheritance, not by Move Tutor or TM.

That made him wonder: Could Ninetales learn it naturally over time?

If he invested a lot of time and resources only for it to fail, that would be a huge waste and could even hurt Ninetales' confidence.

And there was no doubt about Moonblast's power and utility.

Not only did it boast high accuracy and great damage, but it also had a solid chance of lowering the opponent's Special Attack, making it one of the most commonly used Fairy-type moves in competitive battles.

He didn't expect…

That the little one would gradually pick it up from Cresselia.

Ninetales remained serious and focused, and Johan simply watched silently, not wanting to disturb it.

If something blew up, well… this was Lucian's house, after all. As long as no one got hurt, it was fine.

Suddenly—

"Oowoo~!!"

With a long cry, Ninetales' deep blue eyes reflected the full moon above. The energy it had been gathering finally erupted.

Buzz—

A pink energy sphere formed between its nine tails before shooting forward.

Hmm?

Cresselia and Darkrai, locked in battle, both sensed something off at the same time and quickly broke apart, retreating.

Ninetales' Moonblast streaked between them like a shining moonbeam.

Cresselia and Darkrai turned to look at Ninetales.

"Oowoo…"

The little one stuck out its tongue sheepishly and hid behind Johan.

It wasn't intentional! Would they believe that?

Cresselia eyed Ninetales curiously—it didn't sense any hostility from it.

But Darkrai…

It had already failed to drag its target into a nightmare once, and now it had nearly been hit by Moonblast.

"Goka!!"

Charcadet jumped onto the balcony railing and spoke in a deep voice.

Big Boss didn't mean it! Please show some mercy!

Buzz—

Darkrai didn't say a word. A Dark Void orb immediately formed in its hands.

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar narrowed its eyes and licked its lips.

It was curious—how was Darkrai's black sphere different from its own?

It had tried mimicking it earlier, but…

Nope, couldn't copy it.

Maybe if it experienced it firsthand, it would understand?

But before it could act, Johan called out:

"Kubfu, incoming!"

"Ku! zzZ"

The only one who hadn't left the room, Kubfu, heard Johan's call and dashed out, running straight into Darkrai's Dark Void.

Buzz—

A wave of dark energy engulfed Kubfu, swallowing it like a black hole.

Darkrai smirked.

It had never seen a Pokémon rush headfirst into Dark Void before.

Even Cresselia, the guardian of dreams, would start dozing off after being hit—let alone… huh?

Thud—

Kubfu landed beside Charcadet. Under the moonlight, it stood atop the railing… and began shadowboxing!

Whoosh, whoosh—

Its fists and feet blurred with rapid strikes.

Cresselia's eyes widened in surprise. Dark Void… had no effect?

Darkrai was just as stunned.

How was this possible?!

"Ku! zzZ"

Kubfu scratched its head and looked around.

Did something just happen?

Above Darkrai's head, a bright golden question mark seemed to appear.

Was there such a thing as immunity to Dark-type moves?

Even Johan noticed—Kubfu seemed to be Darkrai's natural counter.

And on top of that, Fighting-type moves were super effective against Dark-types.

Cresselia's eyes gleamed with interest as it observed Kubfu.

This little one…

"Goka~"

Charcadet patted Kubfu on the shoulder.

Nice! Just like two days ago when they earned some respect together—now it had saved its dignity once again!

"Ku~(＾＾)zzZ"

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar clenched its fist and smacked its palm.

So Dark Void was just hypnosis, huh?

Since it was hypnosis, then it was of no use to him!

After all, "Johan's Hypnosis" was the best!

Darkrai looked down at its own hands.

For the first time, doubt crept into its mind.

Had it gotten rusty from lack of use?

It spread its hands open again.

This time, instead of Dark Void, it prepared a pure Nightmare-inducing hypnosis.

It was ready to go all out!

"Ka-kaah~!"

Gengar grinned and floated out over the balcony, spreading its hands toward Darkrai.

Come on, let's see whose hypnosis is stronger—"Johan's Hypnosis" or yours!

Darkrai and Gengar locked eyes.

Buzz—

Darkrai: !!!

What the heck?!

Darkrai actually felt its eyelids grow heavy. A wave of sleepiness washed over it.

And worse—

It could sense it was a nightmare.

Wait… who's supposed to be the Lord of Nightmares here?!

At that moment—

Darkrai seriously doubted itself.

Even Cresselia was bewildered.

If Kubfu resisting Dark Void could be explained as a simple miss, then this reverse hypnosis made absolutely no sense.

Cresselia glanced at Johan's Pokémon.

Kubfu, Charcadet, Gengar, Ninetales…

When its gaze met Ninetales', it briefly froze.

"Oowoo?"

Being haunted by Darkrai must be exhausting. How about I give you a nice dream?

Cresselia shuddered, suddenly snapping back to attention and averting its gaze.

What the heck?!

It, the guardian of dreams, had almost been lulled into a dream by another Pokémon?!

This made no sense!

"Garrr~"

Dragonite patted its belly, looking a little bored.

Why wasn't anyone sleeping?

If they slept, it could wake them up!

"Cha!"

Want me to smash them with my hammer?

Tinkaton suggested.

Darkrai and Cresselia instinctively exchanged glances, seeing the same shock in each other's eyes.

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar snickered at Johan.

Hey, do I look just like him?

# 397: Social Etiquette and the Feather of the New Moon

I'm done!

Darkrai felt miserable.

If it continued to "play" any longer, it wasn't just going to lose here—it might even get kicked out of the Darkrai lineage altogether.

With a resentful glance at Johan, Darkrai turned to leave.

"Wait a second," Johan suddenly called out.

"What now?" Darkrai tensed up.

"I'm giving you a box of Pokéblocks." Johan tossed a box into the air. "Consider it an apology."

Darkrai didn't deliberately drag people into nightmares—it was simply its way of gathering energy, part of its "training."

Since it only did this once a year, it was already holding back a lot.

So, giving it some Pokéblocks was a way to make up for it.

Darkrai instinctively caught the box and scoffed.

"Wow, I'm so grateful for this junk."

Johan smiled faintly.

For a wild Pokémon that had never tasted Pokéblocks before, these top-tier ones were quite the temptation.

Don't worry, I didn't put anything weird in them.

"If you like them, how about we—"

"Farewell!"

Darkrai cupped its hands together in a mock gesture of respect, then sank rapidly into the shadows.

As the last wisps of white mist and half of its head remained visible, its voice rang out again:

"Hmph! If you ever want revenge, come find me anytime—I live on Fullmoon Island!"

Cresselia: ???

Revenge?

Revenge for what?

Johan opened his mouth, speechless.

He was just about to ask Darkrai if it wanted to star in a movie. He had written a script for Nightmare Realm and thought Darkrai would be perfect for the role.

He was trying to build a good relationship with it using Pokéblocks…

And it just ran off?

As Darkrai completely disappeared from view, Johan noticed Marshadow in the background, clutching a small stick in its hands and gritting its teeth.

"Marsha! (▼皿▼#)"

That guy just stole my food!

"Oh well, you can run, but you can't hide forever."

First, he needed to finish his tasks and compete in the tournament—then he'd think about his new movie.

His gaze then shifted to Cresselia.

Cresselia shuddered slightly, and a gentle yet nervous telepathic voice echoed in Johan's mind.

[It lives on Newmoon Island.]

"I know," Johan nodded with a smile.

[No, really, it lives on Newmoon Island.]

"I really know."

Johan sighed.

Although Cresselia was the New Moon Pokémon, it actually lived on Fullmoon Island. It was said to only appear on new moons, but since Darkrai often showed up during full moons, Cresselia actually appeared then as well.

Seeing that Johan was certain, Cresselia quietly sighed in relief.

The last thing it wanted was to be minding its own business on Fullmoon Island, only to get dragged into another nightmare.

It had seen what Gengar was capable of.

[Thank you…]

Cresselia finally expressed its gratitude sincerely.

After all, Johan and his team had helped drive Darkrai away, saving it a lot of energy.

[As a token of appreciation…]

A soft, shimmering feather floated from Cresselia's body, landing gently in Johan's hands.

A Feather of the New Moon!

…Another feather?

Johan's mouth twitched. "Thanks, I guess."

Still, it was a "legendary" resource, so its value was undeniable.

[I have imbued this feather with the 'Power of the New Moon.' Use it wisely. I believe you and your… Pokémon will bring peaceful dreams to many.]

As it spoke, Cresselia's gaze swept past Gengar before settling on Ninetales.

At least one of them seemed normal.

"We will," Johan nodded sincerely.

Even without Cresselia saying it, that was already something they strived to do.

That pure, genuine Aura of his made Cresselia's respect for him grow a little more.

[Then I shall take my leave.]

"Would you like a parting gift?" Seeing that Cresselia was about to leave, Johan pulled out another box of Pokéblocks.

Cresselia initially wanted to refuse—it wasn't the type to do good deeds for rewards. Otherwise, it wouldn't silently dispel nightmares every time Darkrai appeared.

But then it remembered the look on Darkrai's face when it opened its box…

[Much appreciated.]

Raising its crescent-shaped head, Cresselia let out a soft hum as it ascended into the night sky, leaving behind a brilliant trail of starlight.

These scattered lights drifted over Canalave City, dispelling every lingering shadow of nightmares.

At the same time, the box in Johan's hand was enveloped in psychic energy and vanished in an instant.

"Ka-kaah?"

They're all gone?

Gengar scratched its head.

Not a single fight? That was two whole boxes of Pokéblocks down the drain…

Johan chuckled. "Understanding the way the world works is a kind of knowledge. Social etiquette is an art. And among Pokémon, the same rules apply."

The little ones all looked confused.

Why did he suddenly start speaking so profoundly?

Johan sighed and simplified his words:

"When they accept our gifts, they'll owe us a favor. Next time we need something, they won't be able to refuse. Like, say, teaching Ninetales a new move or ability."

Ohhh~~

Now they got it.

"At the very least, the next time we meet, they'll show us some respect."

"Goka~~"

Charcadet had learned something today.

So respect isn't just earned through strength—it can also be traded through favors.

Got it.

"Well, do you like it?" Johan held up the Feather of the New Moon for Ninetales.

This thing was perfect for it.

[Feather of the New Moon: A feather that glows like moonlight. Said to possess the power to dispel nightmares. (Blessed by the Guardian of Dreams, the New Moon's Gift)]

It wasn't bad.

At the very least, it would enhance Ninetales' Hypnosis and might even boost its psychic abilities.

"Oowoo~ o(〃'▽'〃)o"

It was clear—Ninetales was pleased.

"It does look nice." Johan tied the feather to a string and hung it around Ninetales' neck.

Surprisingly, the soft green hue of the Feather of the New Moon actually suited it well.

A bit of green never hurts.

"Oowoo~"

Ninetales lifted its head proudly, showing off its new accessory to the others.

"At least it's real."

In the anime, Team Rocket once forged a "Feather of the New Moon."

According to Meowth, it was actually just a Pidgey feather, bent slightly and coated with glow-in-the-dark green paint.

It looked exactly like the real thing—so much so that even Officer Jenny couldn't tell the difference.

But now that Johan had that thought in his head…

He couldn't unsee it.

Case closed.

Cresselia is just a Pidgey evolution.

Shaking his head, he pushed the ridiculous thought aside.

Tonight had brought another gain—

Ninetales had learned Moonblast!

With Moonblast as a single-target move, Dazzling Gleam as an AoE attack, and Misty Terrain as a team-wide buff, Ninetales now had the holy trinity of Fairy-type moves.

Time to slay some dragons.

That was the biggest win for Johan.

After all, it had learned the move for free!

No TMs, no expensive Fairy-type resources—

Free things are the best.

"Oowoo~"

Ninetales yawned, looking pitifully at Johan.

Tired…

The Feather of the New Moon was already doing its job?

"Alright, bedtime." Johan gestured.

The little ones all filed inside.

But halfway through, Johan stopped, narrowing his eyes.

"Gengar."

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar tilted its head.

Johan's lips curled into a very sinister smile as he whispered something in Gengar's ear.

Moments later—

"WHAT THE HELL?! HOW DOES GARCHOMP KNOW DRAGON DANCE?! S-STAY BACK!!"

Lying in bed, hugging Ninetales, Johan smiled in satisfaction.

Revenge is a dish best served immediately.

The next morning at Wake's restaurant, Flint sat at the dining table with dark circles under his eyes, tearing apart a piece of bread lifelessly.

"You're up early, Flint."

Wake, feeling refreshed and in a good mood, finally "remembered" his presence.

Flint looked around, then lowered his voice and asked, "Wake… do you think Garchomp can actually learn Dragon Dance?"

Wake gave him a strange look.

"Why don't you ask Cynthia's Garchomp and find out?"

# 398: Teacher Johan: I’m Wiping the Floor!

Sinnoh, Canalave City, Wake's house.

Bam! Bam! Bam bam bam—

Kubfu unleashed a flurry of punches against a specially made wooden post.

It didn't quite remember what happened last night—only that it had slept incredibly well.

But lately, Kubfu had been training harder than ever.

Not just because it wanted to improve faster, but because it had been a long time since nature had "blessed" it.

Were we not working hard enough?

Bam bam bam—

"Ku~!"

I'll work even harder!

"Cha~!"

Tinkaton approached Kubfu.

Kubfu looked at it in confusion as Tinkaton pulled out… a pair of knuckle dusters from behind its back!

"Ku?"

Kubfu blinked at the weapon.

Is this for me?

Tinkaton grinned, slinging its hammer over its shoulder and nodding firmly.

"Cha~!"

A true warrior must have their own signature weapon. These knuckle dusters would one day strike fear into opponents everywhere!

Kubfu carefully reached out and brushed its fingers against the sharp protrusions of the weapon.

Hiss—

Sharp!

"Ku!!"

Excellent craftsmanship!

With this weapon in hand, how could I not achieve greatness?!

Kubfu's eyes gleamed as it slipped the knuckle dusters onto its fists. Its entire aura changed, sharpening instantly.

Then, it turned toward the wooden post and threw a punch.

CRACK!!

The spikes on the knuckle dusters pierced straight through the wood.

Flint, who happened to be walking by, saw this scene and instinctively touched his face.

That weapon… that's gotta be cheating, right?

Johan, who was reading a book nearby, also twitched at the sight.

Come to think of it…

Does the League have any rules against Pokémon bringing their own weapons into official battles?

"Johan."

Flint walked over.

"Wake says the plane is ready."

"Got it."

Johan closed his book, clapped his hands, and called over the Pokémon playing in the garden.

It was time to head to Lily of the Valley Island!

The little ones had been waiting for this—each of them was full of energy and excitement.

For glory! No—wait, for battle!

January 16th, Noon

Johan and Flint boarded a plane to Lily of the Valley Island.

Wake had other tasks to handle and wouldn't be joining them, though he promised he'd catch up in a few days.

As soon as Flint got on the plane, he fell asleep—probably because he didn't get much rest last night.

Johan, on the other hand, scrolled through the latest League news.

When he entered the official League website, the first thing he saw was:

"Teacher Johan Confirmed to Compete! Want to Challenge Him? Come to the Lily of the Valley Conference!"

Clicking on it, the comments section was filled with:

"He's here! That guy is actually here!"

"I refuse to believe anyone hasn't heard of Teacher Johan's victory at the Indigo Conference!"

"This is our chance to make a name for ourselves—defeat Teacher Johan and win the Lily of the Valley Conference!"

"Just one win! If I win one match, the village chief promised to let me marry his daughter! (Image of the chief's daughter)"

"Wait! @Grain in the Field, Teacher Johan, let him win! I wanna see if he actually follows through!"

"Let him win! LET HIM WIN!"

The online jokesters were as lively as ever.

Johan smiled.

His inner chuuni spirit was starting to flare up.

So, he simply typed a three-word reply:

"I'm wiping the floor!"

Evening

The plane cut through the clouds and arrived at the lush and verdant Lily of the Valley Island.

An independent island off the coast of Sinnoh, it wasn't small—this was where the Sinnoh League headquarters was located.

As the plane landed at the island's airport, the grand League headquarters building could be seen in the distance, along with the massive stadium and the players' village.

The tournament grounds were similar to the Indigo Plateau's setup—main stadium, secondary arenas, market district, and the players' village.

The difference was that…

When Johan arrived at Indigo Plateau, it was already the opening ceremony. But this time, the Lily of the Valley Conference wouldn't start for another two days.

Still, plenty of trainers had already arrived, and vendors had long since set up their stalls.

The tournament format also differed slightly from the Indigo Conference.

The Indigo Conference started with a 256-player bracket, while the Lily of the Valley Conference began at the Round of 64—preliminary matches weren't broadcast.

That was mainly because the Indigo Conference was the first official Pokémon League tournament and had a much larger number of participants.

But in Johan's memory, what he remembered most vividly was the Lily of the Valley Conference from the Diamond & Pearl anime.

After that, starting from Black & White, the quality of League tournaments in the anime started going downhill.

In Diamond & Pearl, the Lily of the Valley Conference was the last tournament that truly focused on strategy and tactics (excluding the "Legendary User").

It was the final showcase of strategic battling in the anime—also the only time Ash used his past Pokémon in a League tournament.

After that, Ash's tactical ability and battle awareness reset to zero, replaced entirely by "bond power-ups."

And then, in one of those "bond battles," he lost to the infamous Water Shuriken spam, a defeat that's still criticized to this day.

"I wonder how many tactical players I'll run into this time…" Johan murmured.

Compared to aggressive fighters like Flint, he preferred facing strategists.

"Huh?"

Flint, who was walking beside him, didn't catch what Johan was saying.

"Nothing," Johan shook his head. "I was just saying you look great."

Flint paused—then burst out laughing.

"Hahaha—see? You've got an eye for talent, Teacher Johan! You're the best judge of character!"

Johan looked up at the sky.

"Teacher Johan, what are you looking at?"

"I'm just worried about getting struck by lightning."

"Lightning? But the weather forecast says it's clear today."

"You don't understand," Johan sighed. "I just told the biggest lie of my life—I'm afraid Arceus might punish me."

Flint: Σ(дlll)

The two arrived at the tournament grounds.

The roads were well-connected, decorations hung everywhere, and colorful balloons floated in the air—it was already a lively scene.

Even though the tournament hadn't started, the grounds were packed with trainers and their Pokémon.

Johan summoned his own Pokémon.

"Oowoo~"

Ninetales immediately wrapped its tail around Johan's hand.

Then, Johan led his Pokémon through the market district.

It had been a while since he last took them out for a shopping trip.

Before long…

Gengar was munching on a bucket of popcorn, sharing it with Dragonite, while Johan stopped at a gift shop with Ninetales and Charcadet.

"Looks like it's almost time for the little one to hatch—I should prepare a gift."

"What about this one?" Johan picked up a pink scarf and showed it to Ninetales.

"Oowoo~ (＾＾)"

Pretty!

Ninetales nodded eagerly.

"Goka."

Charcadet, holding the egg, hesitated.

What if it's a boy?

"Good point."

Johan picked up a few purple scarves instead.

"This one, then!"

Purple?

Ninetales and Charcadet both looked confused.

Isn't that still a "girly" color?

"You guys don't get it…" Johan handed the scarf to the store clerk.

"My sister says purple has elegance."

Both Pokémon's heads filled with question marks.

Just then—

"Move aside! Move aside!"

A pair of loud voices rang out, drawing irritated looks from nearby shoppers.

"What are you looking at?!"

A trio entered their view.

The two in front—one man, one woman—wore black vests with skull emblems and matching skull-patterned bandanas.

Behind them, walking like he owned the place, was a young man with wild white hair, a black-and-white outfit, and a thick gold chain with a skull pendant around his neck.

Johan narrowed his eyes.

Guzma?!

The leader of Team Skull from Alola?

What's he doing here?

And why was he acting so high and mighty?

Johan's eyes narrowed slightly.

Guzma?

The leader of Team Skull from Alola?

Why was he here?

And why was he acting so high and mighty?

"Whoa, that white-haired guy is so arrogant!" Flint, standing beside Johan, couldn't help but comment.

Johan glanced sideways at him.

Bright red spiky hair, hands in his pockets, walking with almost the exact same swagger as Guzma…

Aside from not having a couple of underlings, the two of them were practically identical.

Was that a complaint…?

Or was Flint actually jealous?

If Johan and Wake were leading the way for him like those Team Skull grunts, Flint would probably think he looked even cooler than Guzma…

And honestly, he would think that.

Noticing Flint's gaze, Guzma turned his head toward them.

When he spotted Flint's appearance, he let out a mocking chuckle.

"Tch."

That little sound instantly set Flint off.

"Oh, that does it!" Flint rolled up his sleeves.

"Johan, don't stop me! I have to teach him a lesson today!"

Two seconds later, Flint glanced back at Johan.

"Wait—why aren't you stopping me?"

Johan, who had been busy adjusting his Rotom Phone to get the best camera angle, blinked in confusion.

"Go ahead."

Flint: "…"

"Talk big, but no action…"

Mid-sentence, Johan suddenly shouted, "Gengar! Marshadow! What are you two doing? Get back here!"

Behind Guzma and his two underlings…

Gengar and Marshadow, the latter still holding a little stick, were sneaking up behind them.

At Johan's call, Gengar reluctantly floated back.

Marshadow immediately dropped the stick and slipped into the shadows.

"Ka-kaah~"

That guy definitely isn't a good person.

Capturing him might even come with a bounty.

Johan's eye twitched.

Guzma wasn't exactly a good guy…

But compared to people like Cyrus, Archie, and Maxie, he wasn't that bad.

In the Pokémon anime, after Team Skull, the so-called "villainous teams" of later regions—Team Yell and Team Star—became downright harmless.

Calling Team Skull a "villainous team" was already giving them too much credit.

They were more of a rowdy street gang than anything else.

Wait…

Could Guzma be here to compete in the Lily of the Valley Conference?

The thought suddenly popped into Johan's head.

"You guys staring at something?"

A familiar voice rang out beside him.

Turning toward the source, Johan was surprised to see—

Cynthia.

"Big Boss?" Flint looked equally shocked.

"I thought you were still caught up in your research project?" Johan asked.

Cynthia grinned mischievously.

"This time, my 'homework' is on Dandelion Island, right near Lily of the Valley Island."

Dandelion Island?

Oh, right—there was a place like that.

"Let's go," Cynthia gestured.

"Go where?"

"The League building. The reward we talked about before—you have to pick it up there."

League reward?

Johan's shoulders tensed slightly.

As long as it wasn't another Pokémon Egg, anything was fine.

----------

# 399: Gengar Gets Another Epic Power-Up

Sinnoh League Headquarters Building.

Johan, Cynthia, and Flint entered together.

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar emerged from Johan's shadow, looking around the grand lobby.

Gotta admit, this place was pretty impressive.

It was the first time Johan and Flint had been here, but Cynthia seemed familiar with the place and led them straight to the rewards desk.

"Wait… is that Teacher Johan?"

Their arrival immediately drew some attention.

"And Cynthia, too!"

"Who's the red-haired guy with the explosive hairstyle?"

Walking past the lobby and down a long, polished corridor, they arrived at the rewards collection area.

After submitting Bertha's report, a League staff member immediately retrieved a box that had already been prepared.

"Miss Cynthia, this is what Bertha requested to be delivered," the receptionist said quietly.

Her eyes kept sneaking glances at Johan's face.

Teacher Johan really is handsome… and he looks so good with Miss Cynthia…

"What's inside? Why so formal, and why did we have to come to the League building to get it?"

Johan eyed the neat, rectangular box curiously.

Seeing that it wasn't an incubator for a Pokémon Egg, he let out a huge sigh of relief.

Flint, just as curious, leaned in to get a better look.

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar sniffed the air, its eyes lighting up as if sensing something. It leaned in, too.

Cynthia smiled mysteriously. "Open it and see for yourself."

So secretive.

Johan opened the box and found…

A jagged, irregular stone, about the size of half a palm.

"What the…" Johan froze.

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar's jaw dropped—it looked like it had just seen the most delicious food in the world.

"This is—!" Flint gasped.

"You recognize it?" Johan turned to him.

"Uh… nope." Flint scratched his head. "Just trying to match the mood."

Cynthia chuckled and patted Gengar on the head. "It's quite fitting for you."

Gengar nodded enthusiastically.

"So, Big Boss, what is this thing, exactly?" Flint asked impatiently.

Cynthia's expression grew slightly more serious.

"To be honest, I can't say for sure. But after an evaluation by my grandmother and the League, we've confirmed that it has ancient origins and is connected to the Adamant Orb and Lustrous Orb."

She paused, then continued:

"My grandmother suspects it may be related to the legendary third orb—the Platinum Orb!"

Adamant Orb, Lustrous Orb, and Platinum Orb?

Flint looked completely lost.

Sinnoh had plenty of myths—Arceus, the Time and Space Dragons Dialga and Palkia, and even the Lake Guardians—but there was hardly any mention of Giratina.

Even the so-called "third orb," the Platinum Orb, remained completely missing.

"Though it's incomplete, this is still a legendary-grade resource. It can be used to enhance Ghost-type Pokémon's abilities."

The Adamant and Lustrous Orbs were connected to Dialga and Palkia, respectively.

And the Platinum Orb was tied to Giratina.

That meant it was perfect for Gengar.

But Johan knew—this wasn't the Platinum Orb.

It was—

[Great Platinum Pearl (Fragment): A dazzlingly radiant pearl. When used by Giratina, it enhances its power and allows it to change forms. (A relic from the Hisui era. Beware—Giratina may come looking for it!)]

…Seriously?

After all that secrecy, it turned out to be a Great Platinum Pearl fragment?

And what's this about Giratina "coming for it"?

Giratina's practically already been scammed into submission!

And it's only a fragment?!

What was he even supposed to do with this?

Johan sighed.

Still… judging by Gengar's reaction, this thing clearly had a strong pull on it.

Given Gengar's connection to Giratina, and how Giratina seemed to favor it, maybe this pearl fragment could help it learn something new.

Something like…

"Shadow Sneak+"—the advanced version of Phantom Force. Stronger, faster, and ignores Protect moves.

"Not a fan of it?" Cynthia noticed Johan's hesitation.

Johan forced a smile. "No, I like it. And Gengar definitely likes it."

"Ka-kaah! Ka-kaah!"

Gengar drooled over the pearl fragment, eyes locked onto it.

Fine.

This little guy already chewed on Dusk Stones and licked Toxic Orbs for fun—this fragment should keep it entertained for a long time.

At least it'd save him some money on Dusk Stones and Toxic Orbs.

Saving money = making money.

And most importantly…

At least it wasn't another Pokémon Egg!

With that thought, he suddenly felt much better.

"Good," Cynthia smiled again.

The reason she had been asked to personally deliver the reward was so she could explain its value and express the Sinnoh League's recognition and gratitude toward Johan.

Honestly—

The League had only managed to obtain this thing thanks to Bertha's insistence.

Professor Laventon and Cynthia also played a role in pushing for it.

Sure, Cynthia knew Johan was short on money.

But money could always be earned—or even borrowed if needed.

Something like a legendary-grade resource couldn't just be bought on demand.

With Johan's permission, Gengar wasted no time—its tongue snapped out, wrapped around the pearl fragment, and pulled it straight into its mouth.

"Ka-kaah!"

A shiver ran through its entire body.

Then its eyes narrowed in concentration.

"Ka-kaah~"

A thick black mist erupted from Gengar, swirling around it.

For a brief moment, its body flickered—turning ethereal before solidifying again.

When it reappeared, it was holding two Shadow Balls.

"Ka-kaah!"

Oh yeah, this feels amazing!

And just like that—

Wave Ninja Gengar had gotten another epic power-up.

The legendary Flying Thunder God Technique was likely entering Phase Two!

Gengar tossed the Shadow Balls into its mouth.

Flint stared, completely dumbfounded.

So…

Was anyone gonna explain what this thing actually was?

Night fell.

Cynthia soaked in the bath, letting the warm water wash away her exhaustion.

Bubbles drifted around her, sliding across her smooth, porcelain skin.

Garchomp stood against the wall, arms crossed, sharp eyes scanning the room—guarding against something.

Meanwhile, Cynthia held her phone, speaking into it.

"Yes, I gave it to him."

On the other end of the call was Bertha.

"Did he understand what it was?" Bertha asked.

"I think so. Johan's well-versed in mythology. When I mentioned the third orb, he immediately caught on."

"Of course. He's the one who pacified Regigigas, after all."

Bertha paused, then asked, "Was he happy with it?"

"...Probably?" Cynthia's answer was a bit vague.

Bertha fell silent for a moment before sighing.

"Sigh… I guess, for a trainer, the most enticing reward is still a Pokémon—or an egg, huh?"

She recalled the carefully nurtured Pokémon Egg she had seen on Johan's ship.

"Hmm?"

Cynthia playfully blinked.

Is that what she took from this?

Meanwhile, in his room, Johan's phone rang.

It was Professor Oak.

"Hahaha—Johan! I heard you're competing in another League tournament!"

Oak's hearty laughter echoed through the call.

Johan's expression twitched.

"Professor Oak… you're not coming to watch again, are you?"

"Hm? How'd you know?"

Johan: "…"

"Haha! I'm actually visiting my cousin, who happens to be in Sinnoh for the tournament. Plus, another very talented Pokémon Professor is attending—I wanted to discuss some research with him."

Cousin?

Oh—Samson Oak.

Johan suddenly recalled encountering Guzma earlier that day.

Seemed like a lot of people from Alola were showing up for this tournament.

Tired of sunshine and beaches? Came to Sinnoh for the snow?

As for the "very talented professor" Oak mentioned…

That was probably Professor Kukui.

He and Oak were pretty close.

"So, what's this call about?"

"I received the Regigigas and Heatran data you sent me. I'll be upgrading your Pokédex for you."

Not because Bertha was ignoring you again?

"Much appreciated, Professor."

Upgrading the Pokédex was one thing, but giving Rotom a new "body" was a nice bonus.

Johan put away his phone.

"Alright, everyone—bath time!"

Johan's voice had barely fallen before Gengar—already wearing a pair of swim goggles—jumped straight into the bathtub with a loud splash.

Fortunately, the Sinnoh League had taken trainers' and Pokémon's bathing needs into account, so the bathtubs in the players' rooms were huge.

Otherwise, with just Gengar in there, nobody else would be able to take a bath.

That said…

Even with such a large tub, Dragonite couldn't fit anymore.

Instead, it had to sit on a little stool next to the bath, amusing itself by playing with bubbles.

Meanwhile, Johan stood behind it, holding a massive scrubbing brush, scrubbing Dragonite's back with effort.

"Draaa~ Draaa~~"

Yes, right there, right there! Ahh, a little harder~~

Kubfu, having returned to its natural, "scrawny" form after soaking in the water, emerged from the bath and wiped its forehead with both paws.

"Ku?"

Is it just me, or is the water a little too hot today?

Johan reached in and touched the water.

Hiss—!

"Charcadet, are you trying to turn this into a hot spring?!"

"Goka?"

Charcadet, who had been floating lazily in the water with its eyes half-closed, suddenly jolted awake.

It had gotten too comfortable and had forgotten to regulate its heat output…

"Oowoo?"

Ninetales, who had been standing outside the bathroom door, hesitant to enter, heard the commotion and immediately blew a breath of icy air inside.

Crackle—!

A layer of frost instantly covered everyone in the room, turning Kubfu into a sneezing machine.

Rustle, rustle—

The Pokémon Egg in Charcadet's arms trembled slightly, shaking off the thin layer of ice that had formed over it.

Egg: Surviving in this household is a miracle in itself…

"Oowoo…"

Ninetales stuck out its tongue apologetically, but before Johan's "evil claws" could grab it, it turned tail and bolted.

Johan shook his head.

"All grown up, huh? Knows how to be shy now."

"Cha?"

Tinkaton, flicking water off its long ponytail, glanced up.

Shy?

Johan side-eyed it. "Not you. Your real body is the hammer."

After a nice, refreshing bath, Johan changed into a comfortable set of pajamas and crawled into bed with the little ones.

That night, they all slept soundly.

# 400: Pokémon Egg: I’m Blowing Up!

January 17th, early morning.

Johan reluctantly woke from his dream.

Last night, he had dreamed of Cynthia wearing a school uniform, sweetly calling him "Teacher Johan"…

Who could possibly resist that?!

"Seems like the Feather of the New Moon really works. Even sleeping feels better now…"

Swish—

Gengar yanked open the curtains and cracked the window, taking a deep breath of fresh air.

Unlike Johan, Gengar had barely slept last night. The newly obtained Great Platinum Pearl fragment had it completely hyped up and brimming with energy.

Now, it was showing off by flipping the pearl fragment in and out of its mouth like candy.

"Enough playing around. Take Dragonite out for a spin."

"Ka-kaah!" Gengar saluted and flew off.

"Cha~!"

Take me with you!

Tinkaton jolted awake, grabbed its hammer, and ran after them.

Meanwhile, Kubfu was still in the corner practicing its punches. Ever since getting the knuckle dusters, its fists were basically lethal weapons.

One punch—KO.

A true warrior!

After Breakfast

Flint headed to the training grounds in the players' village to find sparring partners.

Since the tournament hadn't started yet, the place was packed. All it took was a quick shout, and challengers flocked in.

A dream come true for someone like Flint.

Meanwhile, Johan, at Cynthia's invitation, boarded a boat with her to Dandelion Island.

Since the Lily of the Valley Conference hadn't begun yet, it was the perfect time to explore.

"Draaa~"

"Garrr~"

Dragonite and Garchomp flew alongside the boat, cutting through the air and leaving shimmering ripples on the ocean's surface.

"Rotom, slow down!"

Johan, looking a little pale, patted the dashboard.

This speedboat felt way too close to flying.

"Trying to race Dragonite and Garchomp?"

"A-Alright, alright, Rotom…"

At Johan's plea, the Rotom controlling the boat finally eased off the throttle, and Johan let out a breath of relief.

Honestly…

Flying, speedboats, racecars… None of that suited him.

Give him a good old-fashioned bicycle any day.

"Wonder when I'll finally be able to freely bike across water…" Johan sighed.

"Hm?"

Cynthia, petting both Ninetales and Glaceon at the same time, tilted her head in curiosity.

"Nothing. Just thought of a certain lazy person."

"Oh." Cynthia nodded cutely, not pressing further.

Before long, thanks to Rotom's initial reckless speed, they arrived at Dandelion Island much earlier than expected.

A lush, green island—most of it was covered in dense vegetation.

Ancient, towering trees dotted the landscape, their roots deep with history.

Few people knew about this island, leaving it mostly untouched. The natural environment was pristine.

And naturally, wild Pokémon thrived here.

That said…

For Johan and Cynthia, this was basically just a winter sightseeing trip.

Of course, there were still some minor inconveniences—like…

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar suddenly opened its mouth wide and swallowed a Voltorb just as it was about to Self-Destruct.

Then—

BOOM!

Gengar's body briefly expanded like a balloon before it casually spit out the Voltorb, now dazed and spiraling with swirly eyes.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar grinned.

Tingly~!

…Are you treating Voltorb like spicy peppercorns now?!

"I get why wild islands have Pineco that use Self-Destruct, but this many Voltorb… That's a first," Johan muttered.

Cynthia chuckled, tucking a strand of hair behind her ear.

"Perhaps, long ago, this island was inhabited."

"Could be."

That would explain the ruins.

"Battery at 300 percent, Rotom!"

Thanks to the generous Voltorb-powered recharging session, Rotom's energy reserves had soared past its normal limit.

Now, it was glowing so brightly that it looked downright holy.

Seeing this, the exhausted Voltorb all Self-Destructed in shame.

For a while—

BOOM! BOOM! BOOM!

Explosions echoed nonstop across the island.

"Are all the Pokémon here obsessed with Self-Destruct?"

First Pineco in the forest, now Voltorb in the clearing…

"Goka~"

Charcadet called out to Johan.

The Pokémon Egg in its arms was trembling with excitement at every explosion.

For the first time, it was shaking so much it was visible.

"It's about to hatch?" Cynthia asked, surprised.

Johan's expression turned odd.

…You're not planning to avoid becoming a hot spring egg by turning into a bomb egg, are you?

As the Voltorb explosions finally settled, the egg stopped shaking.

But both Johan and Charcadet knew—hatching was only a matter of time now.

Charcadet could even feel something hard inside the egg gently tapping against the shell.

At least it hadn't hatched yet.

Johan sighed in relief.

"Dragonite, fall back a bit. Let Kubfu take point."

At this rate, Dragonite was turning into a walking mine detector.

"Draaa~"

Dragonite drooped its head but obediently stepped back.

As they ventured deeper into the island, the wild Pokémon clearly grew stronger.

Kubfu, on the other hand, loved it.

It was practically level grinding in real-time.

Passing through the forest, they reached a rocky area.

"This is…"

Cynthia spotted a broken stone tablet embedded in the rock.

Brushing away the dust, she revealed faded, ancient inscriptions.

Johan couldn't read them, but Cynthia quickly translated:

"The great hero… the people of Shinjo… ten kings…"

Cynthia spoke without much reaction, but Johan's mind immediately jumped.

The great hero… the people of Shinjo… the legend of the ten kings…

This sounds a lot like the Hisui-era myths!

Did the people of Shinjo once live on Dandelion Island?!

"It's too damaged to read more," Cynthia murmured, frowning.

Garchomp carefully dug the stone tablet out, and Cynthia packed it up to bring back to Celestic Town.

"Ku!!"

Kubfu suddenly knocked a Graveler away and called out to them.

A cave entrance.

"Rotom, lights."

"On it, Rotom!"

Bzzz—

Soft electric light illuminated the dark cave, revealing winding tunnels filled with Rock-type Pokémon.

But none of them posed much of a threat.

With Kubfu and Lucario leading the way, and the sound of shattering rocks echoing now and then, they advanced deeper into the cave.

Eventually, they emerged into a basin at the island's core.

Before them stood…

A pyramid-like ancient ruin.

"There really is a ruin here…"

Johan's eyes flickered with recognition.

Hadn't he seen something about Dandelion Island's ruins in the anime?

Though, the way it looked in the show and what they were experiencing now were… quite different.

Dense vegetation covered the cliffs around the basin, and moss and small plants clung to the ruin itself.

Even if someone flew over the island, they'd barely notice it.

But Cynthia didn't rush forward immediately.

Years of archaeological experience told her—most ruins were not safe.

And…

"Johan, doesn't this ruin look a lot like the Solaceon Ruins? Just… smaller."

Hearing this, Johan's mind clicked.

Solaceon Ruins in Celestic Town, Snowpoint Temple in Snowpoint City, and even the Spear Pillar atop Mount Coronet—

All were built by ancient Sinnoh people who worshiped Arceus.

Celestic Town itself was originally Shinjo Village in the Hisui era. The people of Shinjo were the ancestors of ancient Sinnoh's civilization.

But Shinjo Village eventually fell, and its people dispersed.

Maybe… some of them had fled here, to this remote island, and built a miniature version of the Solaceon Ruins.

Could Cynthia's research topic be the Hisui era?

Or… the ancient people of Sinnoh?

Just as they were pondering—

Rustle, rustle, rustle—

The trees above the basin began to shake.

"Ka-kaah!"

"Lucario!"

Gengar and Lucario immediately tensed up, turning toward the source.

Shhhhaaah!!

A raspy roar echoed through the forest.

Then—

RUMBLE—!

A swarm of Golem tumbled down the cliffs—like an ancient rolling boulder trap!

Johan and Cynthia's faces both darkened.

And before they could react—

BOOM!!

The leading Golem Self-Destructed.

The entire basin shook.

Johan wiped away a cold sweat.

Egg: Explosions look fun! Should I try one too?
